His lines on a work page: he does not ask to be poked when left alone (only a foot tap after 40 seconds).
    - Checked: the app's script parses; `eco/live.js` was run in a test browser on a pretend page with a table and buttons: he draws with no background, 7 buttons inside his drawing area but off him still took their clicks, a poke and a knock-over worked, with the bar's class on he is put away and the picture shows, and when his spot is removed he cleans up. **Not seen on the live app, not tried with a finger on a phone, and the hookup inside `ecoSpotsApply` itself was not run (only read).**
  - **The places** (`ECO_PLACES`: key, plain name, usual picture): happy, confused, sad, impatient, angry (the five faces used by pop-ups and bubbles), `think` (the thinking box), `dance` (the dance for the boss), `logo` (the 8-click logo dance), `update` (the green new update box). `ECO_3D(key)` gives the picked picture's address, so **every new place Eco Man shows up should ask `ECO_3D("<a key>")`, and get its own key in `ECO_PLACES` if he should be able to pick for it**. `ECO_FACES.<face>` is read fresh each time; `ECO_MAN_SVG` is made once when the app opens (a new pick shows there after a refresh). The gallery pictures do not have class `eco-man` (so a click picks, not jigs).
  - Checked: the script parses; the tab was built and looked at in a test picture with the app's styles, a place picked and a picture saved against a pretend database. **Not seen on the live app.**

- **Eco Man** is the company mascot: the smiling house with the red roof flexing its arms (Carlo named him Nov 9, 2026: "we will start to use him"). **Use Carlo's own Eco Man pictures**, which he put in his folder `Eco Man` (`strong-house-expressions.zip`): five faces, **happy, sad, confused, angry, impatient**. In `index.html`: `ECO_FACES` / `ecoFace("confused")` are the still pictures (inline SVG, ids start `eco-<face>-`; `ECO_MAN_SVG` = happy), and the moving ones are `eco/strong-house-<face>.gif` in the repo (used in pop-ups; they have a white background). Match the face to what he says: happy when all is well ("I found the PO, lucky you"), confused when he asks or can't tell (unknown part, no PO, no supplier), sad when something went wrong, impatient/angry when someone keeps clicking where they should not. (My first hand-drawn Eco Man is gone.) His bubbles are a soft cream colour, never strong (Carlo: "a subtle colour, not overbearing"). He talks in a word bubble: `.eco-say` (him on the left, `.eco-bubble` with a pointer, "Eco Man says:"). First use: top of Auto update → supplier mail, saying "Send paperwork to supplierpo@hsginc.ca. I check for new ones every 10 minutes and keep you posted. The last time I looked was Nov 9, 2026 at 8:05 p.m." (`data-eco-last`, filled by `supMailShow` from `last_check`; the old "Papers from suppliers … last looked" heading is gone). Use him for friendly messages from the app; the app talks as "I". Clicking the "Hi Carlo, you need to fix up 5 documents" box (which does nothing) makes him pop up over it saying "Don't click here, Carlo. Click below. 👇" (the signed-in person's first name) for 4 seconds (`data-eco-noclick`). Same pop-up on box 2 of a paper when the PO was found (`data-eco-found`): the angry Eco Man saying "Move on, please." (the PO number link still opens the PO). **"The angry rule"** (Carlo's name for it, Nov 9; when he says "use the angry rule" on something, this is it): for a place people should not click, clicks 1 to 4 show the confused Eco Man, clicks 5 to 10 the angry one, and **more than 10 clicks the angry one turned red in a red bubble** (he shakes a little). Counted per place since the page was opened. Code: `ecoAngryRule(key, anchor, lines)` (`ECO_ANGRY_N`, `.eco-pop.eco-red`). **Standing rule: any Eco Man bubble that has no purpose (nothing in it to press, pick or type in) gets the angry rule when clicked** ("Carlo, nothing to do here. Move on below, please."); this is automatic for every `.eco-bubble` without a control inside, so new bubbles get it too. The hello box uses it, and so does Eco Man's bubble at the top of each paper (`data-eco-top`): "Carlo, nothing to do here. Move on to section 1 below. 👇"; box 2 shows the angry one. Both use `ecoPop(anchor, lines, face)` / `.eco-pop`; use it for other places people click by mistake.

## Auto update: supplier mail → supplier lists and POs

- **No Eco Man pop-ups on Auto update when the wrong spot is clicked** (Carlo, Nov 10: "remove the eco man pop ups from the page when the wrong section is clicked"). The click handler above `ECO_IDLE` returns at once for anything inside `article.au` or the new-item form (`.eco-form-wrap`), so on this page there is no nothing-to-do pop-up, no angry rule, no silly rule, no dance from clicks, and no Training "click Next question" pointer. **This overrides the standing nothing-to-do / silly / dance rules for the Auto update page only; other pages (Receivables bank list…) keep them.** Clicking an Eco Man picture still makes him dance in place. Do not add these pop-ups back here unless he asks.
- Step 1 wording (Carlo, Nov 10): "Is this from Target? Because I think it is, but I need you to confirm it." (it used to end "…but I don't know everything, right?").

- **The paper cards are compact now: one whole paper fits one screen** (Carlo, Nov 10: "remove more spaces, compact each section more, I want to see it all on one screen"). This replaces the big gaps and big squares he asked for on Nov 9 (the 300% spaces, the stacked steps, the big app squares) on this page: a thin dark blue top with "Document 3 of 6" and the heading on one line, a small Eco Man title bubble, **steps 1 and 2 side by side**, step 3 full width under them with the small part boxes, and the paper's buttons as small flat buttons on one line. In steps 1 to 3 the small Eco Man pictures are hidden and his words are plain lines. All of it is one CSS block ("Compact paper") placed after the roomy rules, with `!important`; Training (`.train-cur`) keeps the big one-question look. The older notes below that describe big spacing, stacked steps and square app buttons tell how it used to look; do not bring them back unless he asks. "Big when we can" still holds on other pages.
- **The top of each paper is plain words: no box and no Eco Man** (Carlo, Nov 10: "remove this from the box and no eco man"): "From what I can tell, Carlo, we got this paperwork from … / Sent by … · File … / Carlo, please help me! …" are three plain lines under the dark blue top (CSS on `.smc-top`; Training still shows it as his bubble).
- **The paper's heading is in the white part, just above "From what I can tell…"** (Carlo, Nov 10): "Carlo, I need your help with this. I tried 4 times…" (and the other headings, like "Read. It still needs its order number (PO).") is `.smc-headline`, a bold line under the dark blue top. The dark blue top now holds only "Document 3 of 6" and the status tag.
- **The four lines at the top of a paper have one clear set of sizes** (Carlo, Nov 10: "fix the font in this section so it's easier to read it all"): the heading 21px bold; "From what I can tell…" 17px normal weight with only the supplier and the day bold; "Sent by … · File …" 15px; the green ask 18px bold in a darker green (#14703a). Same left edge, same line spacing, a small gap between lines.
- No green arrow square beside the supplier box in step 1 (Carlo, Nov 10: "remove the flashing green arrow button"; `.sm-sup-arrow` is hidden, the words "If it's the wrong one, you can change it here." stay).
- Step 1's **Look at the paper** is a square app button in the bottom right corner of the step, with its hint to its left (Carlo, Nov 10: "move this to the right bottom of question one, make it a square app"; right after, the three buttons in each part box of question 3, Yes add it · Have a look for yourself · Send it to the supplier page, became square apps too; class `apb-sq` keeps a button square inside the compact paper, where the other buttons are small and flat).
- **No "👉 Needs you" tag on the steps** (Carlo, Nov 10: "remove this"; the "✔ OK" tag on a finished step stays). **All square app buttons on a paper are the same size** (104px, `apb-sq`), and the part boxes are wider (about 380px, two across on a normal screen) so three squares fit in a row (Carlo, Nov 10: "make the sections bigger so the new boxes fit and the new app boxes should be the same size").
- **Each paper (email) is an app square first** (Carlo, Nov 10: "make each email an App button that you click to open, then from there you can edit it or hit the help"). Under the hello box: "Click a paper to open it." and one dark blue square per paper (`.sm-tile`, `data-sm-tile`: Document 2 of 6, the supplier, the day, a small tag Needs you / Finished / A problem / Waiting). A click opens that one paper alone (`MAIL.open`, class `smc-open`, `#sm-box.sm-one`), with a grey **All papers** square above it to go back (`data-sm-back`; it also stops Help). The paper stays open through redraws; if it files itself and leaves the list, the squares come back.
- **Read it again is a yellow gold square app in the top right corner of each paper** (Carlo, Nov 10; class `apb-gold`, same size as the other squares). In the code it is still the same `data-sm-read` button in the paper's foot (`.smc-acts`), placed up there with CSS (`.smc-acts-main` is made `position: static` so the corner is the paper's, not the foot's), so its click handler is unchanged; in Training it stays with the foot.
- **Training is a blue square just left of Read it again, one on every paper, and it does that one paper only** (Carlo, Nov 10: "move the training button next to the read it again button and when pressed have it only do the one part, each part would have its own button"). The 🎓 Training button in the top bar is gone. `data-train-one` → `trainSet(true, paper id)`; `TRAIN.only` (kept in localStorage `reid.training.only`) limits `trainSync` to that paper; the bar says "Document 3 of 6 only · Question 2 of 7"; at the end "That is all for this paper, Carlo. Good job!" (Start again / Stop training); if the paper leaves the list, training turns off. Where the Training note above says "the 🎓 Training button in the top bar" and "after the last paper", read this instead.
- **The paper's Training square is called Help now** (Carlo, Nov 10; ❓ Help; in the code it is still `TRAIN` / `data-train-one`). Its buttons are **Back · Next step · Cancel** (they were Back · Next question · Stop training) and the bar says "❓ Help · Document 3 of 6 only · Step 1 of 10". On the first step the top of the paper says only "We got this from Target." / "I need you to help me find or add the parts now." (no unknown parts: "I need you to help me with it now.") and the three buttons sit **inside that bubble** (`.train-in`; the bar keeps only the count; `TRAIN.topWas` puts the page's own words back in `trainClear`). The other steps keep the buttons in the bar at the top. The new-item form's own one-at-a-time buttons still say Next question.
- **Help, step 2 with no PO found: a "View the document" square in the pop-up** (Carlo, Nov 10), with "Not sure? Open the paper and look for the order number (PO)." It presses the paper's own Look at the paper (`data-sm-open`); it is added by `trainSync` (`.train-add`) and taken off by `trainClear`. Not shown when the PO was found.
- **Read it again does not ask to move on to the next paper** (Carlo, Nov 10): its result box has one button, OK, which closes it. The "Let's do the next one" button described further down is gone.
- **Clean words on the compact paper** (Carlo, Nov 10: "clean up the text writing, it's hard to read and follow"): plain words are one size and not bold; only the question, or the thing to look at, is bold. In step 1 the question comes first, then the supplier box, then Look at the paper. Each part box shows its facts on their own lines with a small label (`.sm-unk-facts`: NAME, COST), then "I can't find this part in our system. Pick one:" (`.sm-unk-ask`), then the three short questions with their box or button. The long Eco Man sentence for a part ("What is this part, Carlo? Because I can't tell…") only shows in Training now.

- **Opening the page fast** (Carlo, Nov 10: "takes too long to load"). The database answers in a few thousandths of a second, so the wait is the trips to it and the size of the app (index.html is about 3.8 MB, loaded again on every refresh). `openAutoUpdate` shows what it already has right away when the page was opened before, then gets the newest in the background (never redrawing while someone types or the new-item form is open); `supMailLoad` asks for everything at once; `supMailRun` does not load it all a second time right after the page did (`MAIL.at`).

- **The mailbox check and "Check again now"** (Carlo, Nov 9: the bubble said "last looked 6:09" at 8:13). GitHub only starts the "every 10 minutes" Supplier mail workflow every 4 to 7 hours, so the database starts it itself: `pg_cron` job `supplier-mail-10min` and the small green "Check again now" square on the last line of Eco Man's bubble (`.eco-mini`: a 32px square with its words beside it, his "25% the size with the text outside") both call `_supplier_mail_dispatch()` (pg_net → GitHub workflow_dispatch of `supplier-mail.yml`). That needs a GitHub key in the Supabase vault (`github_actions_token`; fine-grained, repo reid-app, Actions: Read and write). **An owner pastes it once in the app** (the button shows numbered steps and a box; `supplier_mail_set_key`); never ask Carlo to send the key in chat and never read it back. Until it is saved nothing runs by itself. `ecoCheckNow` / `ecoKeyBox` in `index.html`; RPCs `supplier_mail_check_now` (90 s apart at most), `supplier_mail_check_result` (204 = GitHub took it). `sync/extra/237_supplier_mail_check_now.sql`. (Reminder: any push that touches `sync/extra/` re-runs every extra file for about 4 minutes and the app's buttons can fail meanwhile, so warn Carlo or do it when nobody is working.)
- Each paper's title is Eco Man talking in a word bubble (`header.smc-top.eco-say.smc-eco`): "From what I can tell, Carlo, we got this paperwork from Target on Oct 22, 2026, 6:48 a.m." with "Sent by … · File …" under it, and on a paper with unknown parts a third line in big green letters, "Carlo, please help me! Some of these parts are not in our system yet.", in the same bubble (one Eco Man per paper, not two) (Carlo's wording, Nov 9). Each paper's dark blue heading starts with its place in the list, on its own line: "Document 2 of 5" (`.smc-docno`; Carlo, Nov 9), then the heading. (The small number square that used to sit beside the title bubble is gone: it looked like a second "1" next to step 1.) The top of a paper shows no "Needs you" tag (every paper on the page needs you); the box inside keeps its tag.

- Each document has "📤 Send to <supplier> list". **Send only sends and stays on the Auto update page** (Carlo, Nov 9 afternoon: "I want to stay on the page so I can keep checking them all"; it no longer jumps into the invoice reader). Sent ones have "📥 Load into Supplier invoices", which opens that supplier's invoice reader (Supplier invoices drop-in, `smLoadToInvoices`) to check and Save, so it lands in View Supplier Invoices by month. It leaves the main supplier mail list and goes to "📤 Sent to suppliers" (under it, with Put back) and to the supplier's page tile "📨 Sent from Auto update" (`supplier_mail_send`, `supplier_mail_sent`, `supplier_doc_file`, `sync/extra/229_supplier_mail_sent.sql`).
- **A paper that's done files itself** (Carlo, Nov 9): read (status imported / nothing), supplier known and a PO attached → it goes to that supplier's Sent list with no button press (`supplier_mail_autofile`, called by `supMailRun` and after a PO is attached; sent_by "the app"). Put back by hand = `keep_on_list`, so it stays on the main list until sent by hand. It does NOT open the invoice reader; loading into Supplier invoices is still 📥 Load + Save. `sync/extra/234_supplier_mail_autofile.sql`.
- The filing is done by the database itself, the moment a paper is done, page open or not (`_supplier_mail_file_if_done`, triggers on `supplier_mail_pos` insert and on `supplier_mail` status/supplier change, `236_supplier_mail_autofile_always.sql`). Papers that still need something (couldn't read, needs matching, pick the supplier, no PO) stay on the main list.
- The supplier page tile "📨 Sent from Auto update" turns orange and flashes 3 times every 10 seconds while documents are waiting there (sent, not loaded into Supplier invoices yet): class `sa-wait`, set by `smWaitTiles` from `supplier_mail_sent` counts (Carlo, Nov 9). No number tag on the tile (his earlier rule); the count is in the hover text.
- The main supplier mail list holds only papers not sent yet (`supplier_mail_list` leaves out `sent_at`, `235_supplier_mail_list_unsent.sql`): a sent paper that was then loaded into Supplier invoices used to come back with its Send button, and pressing it looked like nothing was sent.
- The message above the supplier mail list (what just happened: sent, attached, read again…) is big, bold and green and stays in view while scrolling (Carlo, Nov 9; `#sm-box > .staff-msg`). Problems (`.err`) keep their red look.
- **Plain words, big and clear** (Carlo, Nov 9: "use layman terms, some of the people using this may be new to Canada and new to the language"; he also asked me to write to him that way). On Auto update each supplier paper is its own big card (`supMailHtml`, `.smc`, `smPlain`): a numbered title "Paper from Boone", when it came in and who sent it, ONE colour per card on a solid bar across its top that says where the paper stands (green finished, orange needs you, red problem, blue waiting; a colour key sits above the list; Carlo found pale tints "hard to follow"), inside only the box that needs the person is orange with a "👉 Needs you" tag (done boxes get "✔ OK"), then three numbered boxes (1 Who is it from? · 2 Order number (PO) · 3 Prices) and big buttons (Look at the paper, Read it again, Send to … list, Skip this paper, Delete). Short sentences, everyday words, no jargon ("order number (PO)", "our price list", "the app could not read this paper"). Write new screens and messages the same way. Nothing runs off the side of the page (the Sent table below still uses `.sm-sentt` fixed widths).
- **Every click box on Auto update is a square that looks like an app** (Carlo, Nov 9): class `apb` (picture in `.apb-ic` on top, a few words in `.apb-tn` under it; colours `apb-green` go/yes, `apb-blue`, `apb-purple`, `apb-grey` skip/exit, `apb-red` delete; `apb-sm` for the smaller ones inside the boxes). Used for the card buttons, the PO and part choices and the Read it again box. To change a label in code set `.apb-tn`'s text, not the button's. Make new buttons there the same way.
- Card wording and colour (Carlo, Nov 9): all words on the cards are dark (`--ink`), no grey small print. In box 1 and box 2 a small Eco Man asks or says it in a bubble (`.eco-say.eco-sm`): box 1 "Is this from Target? Because I think it is, but I don't know everything, right?" then "If I am right, don't do anything and move on." (the old second line "If it's not, pick the right one above." was taken out when the arrow came in, so it is not said twice) (his wording), and beside the supplier drop-down an arrow pointing at it with "If it's the wrong one, you can change it here." (`.sm-sup-tip`), and under it a **Look at the paper** square (`data-sm-look`, opens the paper like the one at the bottom of the card) with "Not sure? Open the paper and see who it is from."; box 2 "✅ I found the PO: PO … You do not need to do anything.", or "I think it is PO …" / "I could not find the order number (PO) for this paper. Do you want me to make a new PO for it?" with the choice squares inside the bubble. With a PO found it is two lines: "✅ I found the PO: PO 113139572-001." then in big letters "Do not do anything. I found it. Move on." (`.eco-big`). **Each statement is on its own line and a line is never cut in two** (`.eco-l`, nowrap; the boxes are a wrapping flex row so they grow to fit) (Carlo, Nov 9). Carlo asked for Eco Man in the title bubble AND in these boxes, so more than one Eco Man on a paper is what he wants.
- **Friendlier cards** (Carlo, Nov 9 evening: "make it more user friendly"): the top greets the person by first name, "Hi Carlo, you need to fix up 5 documents." (his wording), in a rectangle app box with flare (`.smc-hello`: dark-to-bright blue, waving-hand square, the number to fix in a yellow square, a shine that sweeps across, sparkles; he tried a cloud first and did not keep it; `smMyFirst()` = Staff page first name from `my_started`, else the start of the email). Everything stays square-cornered. Nothing else up there: no explaining paragraph and no Show/filter chips (Carlo, Nov 9: "just show all of them since they all need to be fixed and moved on"). His logic to keep: if everything on a page is the same kind, do not offer a way to sort or filter it, and do not explain what the page already shows. The app talks as "I" and uses the person's first name: a paper with parts it does not know is headed "Carlo, I need your help with this. I tried 3 times to do it myself, but I need your help." (his wording; name from `smMyFirst()`, count from `smTriesOf`). That card shows no "🔁 Tried 3 times" tag and no "Needs you" tag on its top (`smc-said`), because the heading already says both: **never say the same thing twice on a screen, it confuses people** (Carlo, Nov 9). On that card there is no yellow strip: Eco Man's title bubble says "Some of these parts are not in our system yet." The other cards have a yellow "👉 What to do" strip (`.smc-sub`), and the text points at "the orange box", not a box number, because the box that needs you goes full width under the finished (green-edged) ones. **Each part is its own box with all of its sections inside** (Carlo, Nov 10: "5 parts would be 5 boxes"): a thick dark blue frame with a dark blue top that says "Part 2 of 5" and the part number (`.sm-unk`, `.sm-unk-head`), **The part boxes are small and sit side by side so all the parts of a paper fit on one screen** (Carlo, Nov 10, right after: "the sections are too big, make all parts fit on one screen view"): `.sm-unks` is a grid of boxes about 300px wide; inside, the Eco Man pictures and the long explaining lines are hidden with CSS (the same pieces are still there, so the search, Yes add it, Have a look and Send work the same), only the short question and its box or button show; Training still lifts each question big, one at a time. So the long wording below is what Training shows, not the page. In each box Eco Man asks in a bubble (Carlo's wording): "What is this part, Carlo? Because I can't tell." / "The paperwork says the part number is 1/W010-0798, but I can't find it in the system." / "It says its name is PILOT ASSEMBLY SIT COMPLETE and it costs $108.90." / "Help me figure it out." (the name is said once, in the first line) Then three more Eco Man bubbles, one question each (`.sm-unk-opt`): "Carlo, do we have it in our system? Try typing its name in the box below and see if you can find it." with the search box right in the bubble (always showing; type, then click the item) and under the box "If you can't find it, move to the next section and add it." · "Is it a new part? Do you want me to add it to our system?" with a Yes, add it square (`data-sm-new`). After "Sure? Add it", Eco Man asks "Carlo, is this a part, a material, equipment or security?" (four big squares; part = item type `service`) and then opens **the new-item form** (`smNewItemForm`, `.eco-form`): ten numbered questions, big, one under the other, already filled in from the paperwork (name, our code, model/part number, cost, the selling price from our pricing rules, supplier and their part number). **No pricing rule → ask to add one** (Carlo, Nov 9): on question 8, when there is no rule for that kind of item at that cost, Eco Man says "Carlo, I have no pricing rule for part that costs $108.90. Do you want me to add one, so I know from now on?" → Yes, add a rule → type the percent → Save the rule (`pb_rule_add`, `sync/extra/240_pb_rule_add.sql`: the new rule fills the gap the cost sits in, up to the next rule or "and up"; needs `pricebook.edit`), then the price fills in (`noRule` in `smNewItemForm`). Pricing rules are kept per kind (Parts = `service`, Material, Equipment, Security are separate lists on the pricing page); Carlo wants Parts and Material the same, and on Nov 9 I copied his Material rules onto Parts twice, so check they still match when he changes one. **Question 11 is labour** (Carlo, Nov 9; all four kinds): hours and how many techs, optional (empty = no labour). It shows "Labour: 1.5 hours × 1 tech × $55.00 an hour = $82.50. With the part: … total cost." The rate is the one global rate, **Labour cost per hour** on Settings → Pricing control (`company_settings.labour_cost_per_hour`); saved with `set_pb_item_labour` with the rate left empty so the item follows the Settings rate. It does not change the selling price. (The groups for question 4 come from `loadPbCats`, which the form calls itself: they used to be there only if the Price book page had been opened first, so the list was empty and nobody could move on.) **Every question must be answered before it saves; only the picture can wait** (a missed question turns red and says "Question 4: please pick the group it goes in."). Save the new item adds it to the price list right away (`save_pb_item`, `set_pb_item_categories`, `save_pb_item_suppliers` so the next paper matches by itself), not through Approvals. **The picture section** (the one thing that can wait): "Add a picture now" asks "Where is the picture?" → **A file on this computer** (file picker) or **Pick one from the web**: the pictures show **inside the app** (a grid; "Click the picture you want. I will load it for you."; a box to change the words and Look again) and one click loads the picture into the form; it is saved with the item (`set_pb_picture`). How: Edge Function `pic-search` (`supabase/functions/pic-search/index.ts`, deployed; `search` asks SerpApi's Google Images, `fetch` brings a picture back as base64 because browsers can't read other sites' pictures) with a **SerpApi key** in the vault (`serpapi_key`; free plan 250 searches a month, 50 an hour; Google's own search key is closed to new customers and ends Jan 1, 2027). **An owner pastes the key once in the app** (the picture section shows the steps and a box; `pic_search_set_key`); never ask for it in chat. Searches are counted in `pic_searches` (`pic_search_state`). `sync/extra/239_pic_search.sql`. No key yet, the month's searches used up, or a failure → it falls back to the older way: a small Google pictures window with the part number typed, then right-click → Copy image → **Paste the picture** (or drag it in, or Ctrl+V). The paper shows "1 new item added to our price list" (`counts["new item added"]`). (It asks "Sure? Add it" first, because Carlo pressed it by mistake once and I had to undo it by hand: delete the waiting `pricebook_cost_updates` row and its hidden new `pricebook_items` row, clear `done` on the paper's unknown line, status back to `unread`, and un-send the paper) · "Is it not a part at all? Like a courier or shipping charge. Then I can send this paper to Target's page, like a packing slip." then "Have a look for yourself and decide what we should do." with a blue **Have a look for yourself** square (`data-sm-look`, opens the paper) and a green **Send it to the supplier page** square (`data-sm-notpart`; asks "Sure? Send it", marks the line `done` + `skipped`, then sends the whole paper to the supplier's list with `supplier_mail_send`, even if another part on it is still unanswered: Carlo's wording, Nov 9). The button to press next has a yellow "👉 Press this" tag (`apb-next`: Read it again on a problem/unread paper, Delete on an email with no paper). Skip and Delete are smaller, on the right under "Do not need it?".
- **The look of the paper cards is "Look C"** (Carlo picked it from three pictures, Nov 9 evening, after "orange is too strong, hard to look at" and "redo it all"): every paper has the same dark blue top (`--sm-navy` #23415f) and a small tag on the right says where it stands (Needs you yellow #ffd966 with dark words, Finished pale green, A problem pale red, Waiting pale blue; `.smc-bar::after`). Buttons are dark blue squares; green only for send / yes, grey for skip / delete. **Square-ish corners everywhere (4 to 8px): he does not like round "egg" shapes or pills.** No strong orange, no coloured card borders. The screens say "the box marked Needs you". Older notes here that say orange/green/red cards describe the look before this.
- No PO found (no likely PO either): a clear question, "❓ No PO found for this one. Do you want me to make one?" with **Yes, make the PO** / **No, I'll attach it myself**. If one job number is printed on the paper (`supplier_mail_job_guess`) the question names the job and Yes makes the PO for it right away; otherwise Yes opens the job number box (blank = shop stock).
- Attaching a PO by hand ("No, I'll attach it myself" / "Attach a PO by number"): as the number is typed, matching POs show under the box (`po_list` search: PO number, job, customer; number matches and this supplier's first); click one to attach, or Enter when there's just one.
- PO numbers on a grey shaded band (Boone's pick ticket prints Customer PO# on one) weren't read at all by the scan reader. `sync/supplier_mail.py` now reads each page again with the shading washed out (`shaded_numbers`: blur, then black and white at two cut-offs) and adds the PO-like numbers it missed as a line `[shaded parts read again] 155451860-002`; the database ties them (`supplier_mail_ocr_pos`). The same step also reads the page as loose words (tesseract `--psm 11`), which finds numbers typed into form boxes that the line-by-line reading skips (Daikin/Goodman pick ticket IN90539: Customer P.O. Number 155452759). Scans already in with no PO are read again a few per run (`reread_shaded`, mark `[read v4]`), words only, the app's reading is left alone.
- **Read it again scans the page again, in the browser** (Carlo, Nov 9; about 15 s a page): `smScanAgain` does the same steps as the mailbox reader (right way up, plain reading, shading washed out, loose words; adds `[numbers read again] …`), and `supMailRead(row, sup, { rescan: true })` uses those words instead of the saved ones, so a PO or price missed the first time is found and tied (`supplier_mail_link_pos` gets the fresh words). No database change; the saved `ocr_text` is only updated by the mailbox job. The first automatic read never rescans.
- Boone packing slips / pick tickets DO carry prices: list price (5 decimals) and a discount, no net amount. `parseBooneSlip` (index.html) works out cost = list less discount and sends it to approvals (ref `packing slip C448260`, `new_ok: false`); a line whose discount can't be read is left out and said so, never the list price. The form's ruled lines made the discount read as letters ("BO" for 30) and hid boxed PO numbers, so both readers (mailbox `unlined_prices` + sparse reads at other sizes, browser `smNoLines` + `user_defined_dpi`) add `[lines read again without the form's lines]` and more `[numbers read again]`. Mark is now `[read v5]`. Read it again takes about half a minute a page.
- Read it / Read it again opens a square box in the middle of the screen (`smReadAgain`, `.smb`): it shows the reading, then what it found (✅ found it and sent it / 🟠 no PO found / ⚠️ couldn't read), with **Let's do the next one** (reads the next paper down the list the same way) and **Exit** (Carlo, Nov 9). While it works the box shows a clock (⏱ 0:07), and it talks while it waits (Carlo's wording, Nov 9): at 10 s "I know it's taking long, but I am still working." (orange); at 20 s "Wow, this is taking long, but I am trying!" (bigger, green); at 20 s also a drawing of someone trying hard (`SMB_TRYING`) and the box turns green; from 30 s "I hope you know this is not easy. Let me try holding my breath…" (bigger again, purple), a drawing of someone holding their breath (`SMB_BREATH`) and the box turns purple; from 40 s "Do you want to give up for now?" with an **Exit** that stops the reading and leaves the paper as it was (`gaveUp`, `opts.stopped`).
- Read it again was made faster (Carlo, Nov 9: "too long to wait"): the page readers stay ready between papers and up to 3 run side by side (`SM_OCR`, `smOcr`), and the slow extra readings stop as soon as the paper's PO is found (`enough` → `supplier_mail_link_pos`). A number on a shaded band inside a narrow box (Boone invoice C357451, PO 110452200) needs the page washed out AND read as loose words at 200 dpi: both readers do that now (mark `[read v6]`).
- Each paper counts how many times it has been read (`result.tries`, kept in the paper's result; `smTried`): the list shows "🔁 Tried 3 times" beside What happened, and the Read it again box shows "Try number 3" while working and "Tried 3 times" with the result (Carlo, Nov 9). Papers read before the counter started count as 1. The background re-scan doesn't count.
- A paper that still has an unknown part line left stays `unread` on the list even after another line was added or left out (it used to turn `imported` and file itself with a part still unanswered).
- "Couldn't read" (status `unread`) is only for papers a person can still do something about: parts to match, or no PO. A paper with its PO attached and nothing to match is done (`nothing`, note "no costs were checked") and files itself. Dates like 20/10/2026 are day first (Master): `invoiceRefDate` reads a paper day first when any of its dates starts with a number over 12; this was the "date/time field value out of range" problem on Master's emailed invoices (Carlo, Nov 9).
- A document with no PO attached shows its likely PO (`supplier_mail_po_guess`): a printed PO number, or one digit off because scans misread (a highlighter made 106607062 read as 406607062), this supplier first, with Attach. Also "Attach a PO by number" and "+ Make a PO for it" (job number → `get_po_number`, blank → shop stock `new_stock_po`), then attached with `supplier_mail_set_po`.

## Pricing page: "What do you want to price?" (Nov 10)

- (Carlo, Nov 10, 9:10 p.m.: "pricing page is me as well, help make it easier for me"; I listed ten ideas; "do it".) **The math is untouched** (`pfSteps`, `prcRulePrice`, the save calls): only what shows, plus a look at what will change before Save and reprice. `renderPricing`, `prcPartHtml`, `wirePricing`, `wirePrcPart` in `index.html`. No database change.
- **It opens on one question**, "Carlo, what do you want to price?" (`prcWhatHtml`, `PRC_MODES`, `prcSetMode`, `PRC.mode`; class `prc-mode-…` on the article): **🔥 Equipment** (a series) · **🔩 Parts, Material and Security** (markup by cost) · **⭐ Member prices** · **⚙ Company numbers** (labour cost per hour, margin goal, who can print partner books: the two `.prc-more` sections, now only in this mode). Then a row with **⬅ Start over** and **❓ Help** (`PRC_HELP`). A link that names a series or a kind (an item's 💲 Pricing for this, `#pricing=12`, the Tools tab) goes straight to it (`openPricing` sets the mode).
- **Equipment: pick a series from squares** (`.prc-pickser`, `PRC.pickS`; the long side list `nav.prc-list` is gone): grouped by section, with **🔎 Find a series** (`#prc-sfind`, hides the squares that do not match) and the "Make a new series" form under them. The sheet then has the whole width, with **⬅ Pick another series** in its heading.
- **The sheet in three numbered steps** (`stepH` rows, `tr.prc-stephead`): **1 What it costs us** (Equipment, Thermostat or add-on, Material, Warranty, Labour → "What it costs us in all") · **2 What we add** ("Profit we want [35] %" was "Total ÷ (1 − 35 %)"; Owner take home; "So far"; Sales commission; "So far, with commission"; Partner fee; "So far, with the partner fee") · **3 Round it, then the sell price** (Round to the nearest; "Then add $"; Sell price). Same `data-k` boxes and `data-v` cells, so `prcCalc` and Save are unchanged.
- **The money in one sentence** under the sheet (`#prc-plain`, written by `prcCalc` for the model picked, Reid's column): "We pay $3,455.00. We sell it for $6,890.00. After the sales commission ($506.73) we keep $2,928.27."
- **Save and reprice asks first, with what will change** (in the `#prc-save` handler): every model's Reid price now and after, worked out in the browser the same way the sheet does it ("3 of 6 Reid prices will change: 3 go up, 0 go down", a table of up to 15, green ▲ / red ▼), then **Save and reprice** or **Go back, do not save**. The database still does the real repricing; partner prices are not listed, only said to be redone.
- **A yellow bar when something is typed and not saved** (`#prc-dirty`, `prcDirtyN`, `prcDirtyShow`; sticky at the top): "⚠ You changed 2 things. They are not saved yet." with **Save now** (presses the page's own save). It counts each box against the value it was drawn with, plus `PRC.touched` for a line or partner column added or taken off. Leaving with unsaved changes (another square, another series, another kind) asks first (`prcLeaveOk`).
- **Parts, Material, Security**: **1 Pick the kind** (four buttons, `.prc-kind`, the old `data-prc-part`) · **2 Try it** at the top and big ("type what one costs us" → "it sells for $120.00. $40.00 + 200%. We make $80.00. The lit line below set it."; the line that set it is lit yellow, `tr.prc-tryhit`) · **3 The lines** with plain headings **Costs us from · up to · We add · Example** ("One that costs us $50.00 sells for $150.00. We make $100.00."; one `data-rex` cell replaced Gross profit, Markup and Real). Gap and overlap messages use the new words and are big and red ("No line covers items that cost $50.01 to $59.99. The next “Costs us from” should be $50.01.").
- Checked: the script parses; the real page code was run in a test browser with a pretend series and pretend markup lines: the first screen, the series squares, the sheet with live numbers, the yellow bar after a change, the what-will-change box, the parts lines with Try it. **Not seen on the live app, and no real save was made.** Member prices and Company numbers were only moved, not changed.

## A partner's own price book, the invoice page as cards with Help, and the Bank page (Nov 12, late)

- **A partner can have its own price book** (Carlo, Nov 12, 10:08 p.m.: "when a partner is picked then have the Price book option you can pick only come from that book; I made one called vista, connect it to vista for me and have a way for me to do this for partners in the future"). `partners.price_book_id` (`sync/extra/273_partner_price_book.sql`, live Nov 12). **Vista Credit is on the book "Vista"** (book 4; set straight in the database; on Nov 12 it held 2 groups, Hot Water Tanks & Tankless and Water Heaters, 67 items in use). **Where he sets it for other partners: Price books page → open a book → "Which partner uses this book?"** (owners only; tick boxes; a partner has one book; RPCs `partner_books()`, `partner_books_set(book, partners[])`; the book's line in the list shows 🤝 and the partner). On the invoice of a partner's job (`inv_price_book(invoice)` → `I.pbook`; the partner is the job's partner, else who the invoice is billed to): step 1 reads "Add from the Vista price book", **the whole book shows as soft squares** (picture or 📦, name, price, ＋ Add; `#ie-book`, `.ie-bk`; loaded once with `inv_item_search_book(invoice, '', 200)`, kept in `IE.bookItems`), the search box only narrows those squares, and the Price book button (the print book pop-up, which shows everything) is not drawn. `_my_book()` now reads `app.pb_partner_book` first; only `inv_item_search_book` sets it. A partner with no book: the invoice searches everything as before. **Not limited**: "Add a line that isn't in the price book", the booking's Install → Price Book search (Reid only), and estimates. The prices are the book's own prices (its level and %).
- **The partner's price book is one button on the invoice; it opens its own page with pictures; one pick closes it** (Carlo, Nov 12, 10:33 p.m.: "too much on the page for options, make it a click button that will open the page and have it show pictures"; 10:34 p.m.: "when the book opens and I pick something have it close and go back to the invoice; if I need to pick something else then I do it by adding another item"). **Where the note above says the whole book shows as squares in step 1 with a search box, read this.** Step 1 on a partner's invoice is one soft square button, **📖 Open the Vista price book** (`#ie-bookopen`, `.ie-bookbtn`, 230 by 190; no search box on the page). Once the invoice has a line it reads **➕ Add another item**. A click opens the book as a page over the invoice (`ieBookPop`, `#ie-bookpop`, `role="dialog"`): the book's name, a search box (it narrows the cards), **✕ Close**, and one card for each item with its picture (150px; loaded 40 at a time with `pb_pictures`; 📦 when it has none), name, code, price and a green **＋ Add**. One click on Add puts the item on the invoice, closes the book and shows the invoice again with "✓ … is on the invoice. To add something else, press “Add another item”." Escape, ✕ Close or a click on the dark part closes it with nothing added. Looked at in a test picture with six pretend items (no real pictures in the test). **Add itself, the real pictures and the live app were not run or seen.**
- **No "Take the payment" when a partner pays** (Carlo, Nov 12, 10:37 p.m.: "when it's a partner and I finish it should not ask for Take a payment because the partner is paying; the only time that would happen is if we add another visit but do not make it the supplier"). On a finished invoice that is billed to a partner (`I.bill.partner`), or a store job (`I.store`, where the customer pays the store), the payment box is not drawn; a pale green box says "Vista Credit pays this invoice. You do not take a payment from the customer…" (`ppay`, `.ie-ppay`), the last of the four small steps reads "Vista Credit pays", and the Finish message says there is no payment to take. **An invoice on a partner's job that is billed to the customer (his "another visit" case) keeps Take the payment.** I read his "supplier" as the partner. I added store jobs (Costco) myself, since the old box already said you normally do not take a payment there; told him. The office still records partner money on the Partners page. Checked: the script parses. **Not run and not seen on the live app.**
- **Help: white words on a lit box go dark** (Carlo, Nov 12, 10:38 p.m., on the invoice page's Help: "i can not read it cause its white make it black"). The first step lights the page's dark banner (`header.cl-hero`), and the Help wash made it pale with its white words still white. Now a banner or a dark "You are looking at" bar that is lit by Help has near-black words (#10233d) on the pale wash, on every page; the help box's own words are near-black too. **When a Help step points at a dark box, check its words can be read.** Looked at in a test picture of the invoice page's step 1.
- **Help: a typing box is shown with its label** (Carlo, Nov 12, 10:41 p.m., with a picture of step 9 on the invoice: an empty box alone on the screen; "i can not see all the help instructions"). With only one section on the screen, a big text box (`textarea`) was lit without its label, so nothing said what the box was. `pbHelp` now lights the box's whole label for a `textarea` too (it already did for `input` and `select`), on every page; on the invoice the Finish step lights its whole row. The help box can never be taller than the screen (it scrolls inside). **I guessed this is what he meant; asked him to say which step if words are still missing.** Checked: the script parses. Not seen on the live app.
- **Invoice page: square app boxes, the tank's two documents, and a "What is done" list in the right side bar** (Carlo, Nov 13, 8:57 a.m.: "make the Create the P.O. and the credit order into a square App Box; make all the buttons on the page look like that; when I pick the supplier and create the P.O. and Credit Memo have it show up in section 4 as 2 documents you can click rather than the line format; on the right side bar have a tab to change the side bar view to a list of what is done and what is still needed for the call, example 1 Add item from price book, marked Done or Incomplete").
  - **Square app boxes** (`.ie-sq`: a picture on top, a few bold words; soft look): 📦 **Create the P.O. and the Credit Order** and ✅ **Finish the invoice** are the big pale green ones (`.ie-sq-go`, 176 by 150); ✕ Close (top, small), and at the bottom ✕ Close · 🖨 See or print the invoice · ✉ Email the invoice · 👤 Open the customer (132 by 124). **Every other button on the page** (`article.ie .btn`: Add, Record the payment, Use Vista rates, The credit came back…) is in the soft look by CSS; the red "Yes, cancel the invoice" stays red. The ids and clicks did not change.
  - **Section 4 shows two documents** once the P.O. is made (`.ie-docs`, `button.ie-doc`, in `ieTankPoHtml`): **📦 P.O. 116700001-1** (bought from, the tank, its cost; a click opens the P.O.'s page) and **🧾 Credit Order CO-7** with its state on it (⏳ Waiting for the credit, orange top · ✔ The credit came back, green top · Cancelled, grey top). A click on the Credit Order opens its details right under the two documents (`.ie-tco-box`, `IE_TCO_OPEN`: the same words and the ✓ The credit came back / Undo buttons as before). The old green and yellow lines are gone. He wrote "Credit Memo"; the paper made there is the Credit Order (the Credit Memo is made later, when the supplier's credit paper comes in).
  - **The right side bar** (`aside.ie-side`, now on the page for everyone, not only people who see costs): **✅ What is done** (`ieTodoHtml`): a numbered list, each line green "✔ Done" or orange "Incomplete" (or "Waiting"): 1 Add an item from the Vista price book · 2 Type Vista Credit's account number and approval number (only what that partner needs) · 3 Make the P.O. for the tank · 4 The supplier's credit comes back (Vista pays only) · 5 Finish the invoice · 6 The customer signs · 7 Vista Credit pays us (or Take the payment). The top says "5 of 7 still to do." A click on a line goes to that part of the page. People who see costs have two tabs, **✅ What is done · 💲 Job costs** (`data-ie-side`, `IE_SIDE.tab`, kept on that computer in `reid.ie.side`); it opens on What is done. Lines that do not apply to an invoice (no partner, no tank) are left out. **Not in the list: booking the walk or the install** (the invoice does not know the job's visits); say so if he asks, it needs a look-up.
  - **The list and the page are in the order the work is done** (Carlo, Nov 13, 9:05 a.m.: "question 2 on the side bar should be Which supplier did we buy the tank from; why is it not in order"). The list now reads: 1 Add an item from the Vista price book · **2 Pick the supplier we bought the tank from, and create the P.O. and the Credit Order** · 3 The supplier's credit comes back · 4 Type Vista Credit's account number and approval number · 5 Finish the invoice · 6 The customer signs · 7 Vista Credit pays us. **The page's cards follow the same order**: 1 Add, 2 What is on this invoice, **3 The P.O. for the tank**, 4 What Vista Credit needs, 5 Finish (the tank card used to be 4, after the partner's card). Where the notes above give the older order, read this. **Lesson: a list of steps follows the order the person does them, and the page's own numbers follow the same order.**
  - **No "No charge" and no "Hide on the printed invoice" tick boxes on an invoice line, for now** (Carlo, Nov 13, 9:07 a.m.: "also remove these 2 options for now"). They are not drawn under a line any more. **A line that already has one ticked still shows that one box**, so it can be taken off (I chose that, or such a line would be stuck). Nothing in the database changed; to bring them back, draw both whenever `edit` is true (the `.ie-opts` line in `renderInvoiceEditor`). Checked: the script parses. Not seen on the live app.
  - **The partner's rates box is off the invoice page** (Carlo, Nov 13, 9:08 a.m.: "remove this section [Vista job · Vista pays for the tank… Vista rates: labour $100.00/hr · service call $110.00… Use Vista rates] since we can set the rates in the partner page; we want to keep this page clean, simple and easy to use"). `ruleH` is empty when who pays for the tank is known. **The Use Vista rates button is gone with it, so nothing on the invoice page puts the partner's labour and service call rates on the lines any more** (`#ie-prates`' handler is still in the code); told him. Only when the tank answer was never picked at booking, a small "Who pays for the tank?" with its choices still shows, or that could not be answered. Where older notes say "the partner's rates box", read this. Checked: the script parses. Not seen on the live app.
  - **The partner's rates go on by themselves** (Carlo, Nov 13, 9:26 a.m.: "I want the rates from the Partners page to go on by themselves"). `ieAutoRates()` (called in `openInvoiceEditor`, before the page is drawn): on a partner's job's invoice that is not finished, a **labour** line or a **service call** line (the same test the old Use rates button used: the partner rule's own line kinds, else `IE_SVC` / `IE_LABOUR`) is set to that partner's rate from the Partners page (`inv_partner_rule`: `labour`, `service_call`) with `inv_update_line`, then the invoice is loaded again. **Once for each line**: the line ids already looked at are kept on that computer (`IE_RATED`, localStorage `reid.ie.rated`, by invoice), so a price typed by hand on the line afterwards stays. Parts, materials and the tank keep their own price. Nothing is said on the screen; the line just has the rate. **Limits**: it happens when the invoice page is opened or drawn again, not in the database, so a line added some other way has the price book's price until the invoice is opened; and an invoice opened for the first time on another computer has its labour and service call lines set to the rate once more there (a hand-typed price on such a line would be put back to the rate that one time). A rate changed later on the Partners page does not change lines already looked at. The old `ieApplyPartnerRates` and the `#ie-prates` handler are still in the code, unused. Checked: the script parses. **Not run and not seen on the live app.**
  - **Picking the supplier shows in the side bar right away** (Carlo, Nov 13, 9:27 a.m.: "question 2, when I pick the supplier the right side bar should show it right away but it did not change"). Line 2 used to be "Pick the supplier… and create the P.O." and only went green once the P.O. was made. It is two lines now: **2 Pick the supplier we bought the tank from** (green the moment a supplier is picked in the drop-down, or already picked because only one fits; `li[data-ie-todo="tanksup"]`, `ieTodoSync()` reads the drop-down, no reload) and **3 Create the P.O. and the Credit Order** (green when the P.O. is made). The "N of M still to do" line follows. The picked supplier is kept if the page is drawn again before the P.O. is made (`IE.tpoSup`, `IE.tpoFor`, `IE.tpoOther`); it is not saved in the database until the P.O. is made. So the list for a Vista-pays tank is eight lines. Checked in a test page with the real code: pick a supplier, line 2 turns Done and the count drops; the page drawn again keeps the pick; un-pick, it goes back to Incomplete. **Not seen on the live app.**
  - **The page's numbers are the side bar list's numbers** (Carlo, Nov 13, 9:36 a.m., with a picture: the tank card was headed "3" and the steps inside it were "1 Which line is the tank?" and "2 Which supplier…", so a 3 sat above a 1 and a 2: "why is step 3 above step 1 and not after step 2"). There were two sets of numbers on the page (the cards' own 1 to 5, and 1, 2 inside the tank card), and neither matched the list. Now there is **one set**: `ieTodoRows(I, o)` makes the list's rows, each with a key (`add`, `tanksup`, `tankpo`, `credit`, `partner`, `finish`, `sign`, `pay`); `renderInvoiceEditor` reads each key's number into `NO` (`IE.stepNo`) and `H(words, small, n)` shows that number. On a Vista-pays tank invoice the page reads, top to bottom: **1** Add from the Vista price book · What is on this invoice (**no number**: it is not a step) · The P.O. for the tank (**no number** on the card) with, inside, Which line is the tank? (no number) · **2** Which supplier did we buy the tank from? · **3** Press this box (the green Create box) · **5** What Vista Credit needs · **6** Finish. 4 (the supplier's credit comes back), 7 and 8 have no heading of their own on the page while the invoice is not finished. **Rule: one set of step numbers on a page; a number on the page is always the same number in the list; a heading that is not a step has no number; never number steps inside a numbered card.** Looked at in a test picture (1, 2, 3 run down the page beside the same 1, 2, 3 in the list). **Not seen on the live app.**
  - **Every number in the list has the same number on the page** (Carlo, Nov 13, 9:38 a.m.: "step 4 on the page and the side bar do not match; step 5 does not match"; his screen most likely still had the copy from before the note above, where the page's 4 was What Vista Credit needs and the list's 4 was the credit). To leave no gap: the tank card now has a heading **4 The supplier's credit comes back** (Vista pays): before the P.O. is made it says "Nothing to do here yet. First do step 3…"; after, the Credit Order's details sit under it, open (they are no longer hidden until the document is clicked; a click on the Credit Order document still closes and opens them). Once the P.O. is made, heading 3 reads "The P.O. and the Credit Order are made. Click one to open it." over the two documents. On a finished invoice the boxes are headed **7 The customer signs** and **8 Vista Credit pays us** (or Take the payment) with the list's numbers (`NO.sign`, `NO.pay`, `.ie-sec-n-sm`). **The old small row "Add the work · Finish · Customer signs · Payment" is hidden for good** (it had its own 1 to 4, and the list says the same). Checked in a test page with the real code: not finished, the page reads 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6 and the list the same; finished with a P.O., 3, 4, 5 on the page match the list. **The sign and pay boxes were not looked at in a picture, and nothing was seen on the live app.**
  - **Two note boxes in section 1, once the equipment is on the invoice** (Carlo, Nov 13, 9:52 a.m.: "section 1, after the equipment is added, have 2 note spots open: one for notes for job that the customer will see and one notes for techs that the customer will not see"). In the Add card, under the Add button, when the invoice has a line and is not finished (`notesUp`, `.ie-notes2`; side by side when there is room, one under the other when not): **📄 Notes for the job** ("The customer sees this. It prints on the invoice."; it is the invoice's own note, the same `#ie-notes`, so the note box is gone from the Finish card; it is saved as soon as the box is left, `inv_set_notes`, and again at Finish) and **🔧 Notes for the techs** ("The customer does not see this. It is saved on the job."; `#ie-technote`, **Save the note for the techs** → `add_note('job', I.job_id, "For the techs (the customer does not see this): …")`, so it is a job note: it shows in the job's notes on the customer's page; after a save the box empties and "✓ Saved for the techs: …" shows under it). **Limits**: a tech note saved before is not shown again when the invoice is opened later (it is in the job's notes), and it can not be changed from here, only another one added. With no line yet the note box is where it was, in the Finish card. ❓ Help has a step for each. Checked in a test page with the real code: both boxes show, nothing past the card's edge, one `#ie-notes` on the page, a tech note saves and shows under the box. **`inv_set_notes` on a not-finished invoice and the real `add_note` were not run (the database could not be reached from the chat), and nothing was seen on the live app.**
  - **The signature box is only for the tech** (Carlo, Nov 13, 9:52 a.m.: "remove the signature part, it should only show up when the call is being done by the tech"). On a finished invoice "The customer signs" is drawn only when the person signed in is a technician (`signHere = ME.role === "technician"`). I took "the call is being done by the tech" to mean the tech's own screen. For everyone else the box is gone, and the side bar line reads "The customer signs (the tech gets it on the call)" with **Waiting** until it is signed (it still counts, because a partner's invoice can not be sent without it). **An owner or office person can no longer take a signature on the invoice page**; say so if he asks.
  - **Notes for the techs stay on the invoice and on the job; they are never printed and never sent to the customer; more can be added** (Carlo, Nov 13, 9:59 a.m.: "leave tech notes on the job as well, just make sure they can never be printed or sent to the customer, and make it so new ones can be added with same rules"). A tech note is a job note whose words start with `TECH_NOTE` ("For the techs (the customer does not see this): "; `isTechNote(body)`). **On the invoice**: `openInvoiceEditor` reads the job's tech notes (`notes` table, `entity_type` job, body like the prefix) into `IE.techNotes`, and the Notes for the techs box lists them under "Saved for the techs (2). Never printed. Never sent to the customer." with their day; the box under them adds another. After a save the invoice is loaded again, so the new one is in the list. Once the invoice is finished the box is its own box under the cards (`.ie-technotes-box`), so notes can still be read and added. Where the note above says a saved tech note is not shown again, read this. **Kept away from the customer**: the printed and emailed invoice only carries the invoice's own note (`invoices.notes`), never job notes; the note boxes do not print (`@media print`); and `nfNoTech` takes tech notes out of the notes offered for a partner's list (both `partner_file_notes` calls: the partner file page and the HTIN List), so they can not be ticked and sent to a partner's portal either. **On the customer's page** a tech note in Job notes has a small sand tag, "🔧 For the techs only. Never printed. Never sent to the customer.", and its words show without the prefix (`noteHtml`, `.note-techonly`). **Rule: any new paper, email, text or export that goes to a customer or a partner must leave out `isTechNote` notes.** A tech note can still be changed or deleted from the customer's page like any job note (if the prefix is edited away it becomes a plain job note). Checked in a test page with the real code: two saved notes list on a not-finished and on a finished invoice, nothing past the edge, the helpers tell a tech note from a plain one, the partner filter drops it, the customer page tag shows. **The real read from the notes table and the real save were not run (the database could not be reached from the chat), and nothing was seen on the live app.**

  - **A cancelled invoice or job also cancels its P.O. and its Credit Order** (Carlo, Nov 13, 11:55 a.m.: "when a job or invoice is cancelled it should also cancel the P.O. or Credit Memo"; the paper made with the tank's P.O. is the Credit Order, so that is what is cancelled). Done in the database with triggers, so it happens whichever button cancels (`sync/extra/278_cancel_follows.sql`, live Nov 13): **an invoice made void, or cancelled and removed** → the tank P.O. made from it is cancelled and its Credit Order that is still waiting is cancelled (`trg_invoice_cancel_follow`, `trg_invoice_delete_follow` → `_cancel_tank_orders`). **A job cancelled, or deleted** → every P.O. of the job that is not received yet is cancelled, and its waiting Credit Orders (`trg_job_cancel_follow`, `trg_job_delete_follow`; a job turns cancelled by itself when its only call is cancelled). Each one writes a job note ("PO … cancelled: invoice … was cancelled.", "Credit Order CO-… cancelled: …") and a line in the history; the P.O. is kept on file as Cancelled, not deleted, and can be put back from its page. **What I chose**: a P.O. that is already received or billed is left alone (the parts are here) and a job note says so; a Credit Order whose credit already came back is left alone; cancelling an invoice only touches the tank P.O. made from that invoice, not the job's other P.O.s. **The past**: the 20 Credit Orders and tank P.O.s whose invoice was already cancelled (CO-3 to CO-22, all from his tests of Nov 11 to 13) were cancelled the same way, so they are off the Tasks page's waiting list. ## Price book page (the list): made simple (Nov 10)

- (Carlo, Nov 10, 9:03 p.m.: "price book page is very complicated… I use it and it hurts my head"; I listed ten ideas; "do it all". He did not say if he mostly finds one item or goes through groups, so it opens on the search and the group tree is closed.) Nothing about prices or how the list is asked for changed; `renderPricebook`, `pbCard`, `pbCatTree` in `index.html`. No database change.
- **Top of the page, in this order**: **🔎 Find an item** (a big search box, the same `#pb-q` / `#pb-filters`, with the cursor in it when the page opens: `PB.qFocus` in `openPricebook`) with **⚙ View** and **❓ Help** squares on its right · **What needs you:** four boxes with counts (`pbNeedsHtml`, `pbNeedsLoad`, `PBNEED`, `PB_NEEDS`: 🧾 New costs waiting = `PB.upd.size`, opens Auto update → Approvals; 📏 Off the markup rule, 📁 In no group, 🖼 No picture = the list's own flags `off_rule`, `no_category`, `no_picture`, counted with three `pb_list` calls of one line each, at most every 10 minutes, people who edit only; a click shows that list, a second click goes back) · **five kind squares** (`.pb-quick .pb-kind`: Parts, Material, Equipment, Security, Show all; the picked one has a yellow frame; they replace the "… only" buttons) · **📁 Pick a group** · the dark blue **"You are looking at: Parts · in use · “igniter” · 1,204 items"** bar (`.ow-sortbar.pb-now`; it still holds the `<strong>` the search writes "Searching…" into) with the group's path as a white tag with ✕ to leave it, **↺ Start over** (`#pb-reset`: every kind, in use, every group, no search, no special list), **More columns / Fewer columns** (list only) and Pick all shown.
- **⚙ View box** (`PB.viewOpen`, `.pb-viewbox`, opens right under the search): the drop-downs that used to sit in the first group heading: Kind (it has Fees and Labour, which have no square), Status, Across (1 = list, 2 to 6 = pictures), Order, Only; View as; and the colour key. `PB.filtersHtml` is emptied after it is built, so group headings carry no drop-downs.
- **The group tree is closed until asked for** (`PB.catsOpen`, no longer open by itself on wide screens): closed, it is one button and the list gets the whole width (`.pb-layout:has(> .pb-cats:not([open]))`); open, it is the left column as before with all its tools. Picking a group with nothing inside it closes the tree again; a group with groups inside stays open.
- **The list (Across 1) shows four things**: the item (name, item number, supplier), **Sell price**, **What we pay**, **We make** (dollars; "we lose $6.10" in red; "no cost"). Group, Kind, Price for members, Margin and Used are `pb-x` columns, hidden until More columns (`PB.moreCols`, kept in `localStorage pb_morecols`; class `pb-fewcols` on the table). The picture cards also say What we pay and We make.
- **Colour strips** down the left of a line or card, only for people who see costs (`pbFlagCls`, `.pb-bad1..3`): **red** = sells for less than it costs · **amber** = off the markup rule (`pbOff`) · **yellow** = no cost (parts, material, equipment, security).
- **Ticked items: one button**, "❓ What do you want to do with these 5?" (`#pb-bulk-what`) → big choices one under the other: change the price by a percent · set to the markup rule · put in a group or take out · move to another kind · retire (or bring back) · paste the copied picture · take the ticks off. The old bar is still drawn; a choice either presses its hidden button or shows only that one piece of the bar (`PB.bulkMode`, classes `pb-bk-pct / -cat / -move`).
- **❓ Help**: `pbHelp(0, PB_LIST_HELP)`, 10 steps.
- **No counts on the kind squares** (I had offered them): the database has no cheap count for each kind, and five more slow calls on every visit were not worth it.
- Checked: the script parses; the page was built in Node with pretend items in five states (list, more columns, pictures, items ticked, View open with a group and a search) and its tags are balanced (scratch `tpl4.js`); the list, the View box and the ticked bar were looked at in test pictures with the app's own styles. **Not seen on the live app; the picture grid, the open group tree and the item panel beside the list were not looked at with the new top.**

## Price book: 18-2 Thermostat Wire (set up by the foot, Nov 10)

- Carlo: "I bought it for 50 dollars and I got 250 feet, I resell it by the foot." Item 5522 (`182WIRE`, "18-2 Thermostat Wire", material) was set straight in the database: sold by the **foot**, cost **$0.20 a foot** ($50 ÷ 250), feet bought **250** (`unit_length`), price **$0.60 a foot** (what his Material markup rule gives for a $0.20 cost; he can change it), and brought back from retired (`active`). Stock was 10,781 ft, untouched.
- **A cost from a supplier paper is now put by the foot for items sold by the foot** (Carlo, Nov 10: "yes do this"). The paper gives the price of the whole roll ($51.50 from Boone); approving that as it was would have made the cost $51.50 a foot. `_cost_per_foot(item, cost)` (`sync/extra/250_cost_by_the_foot.sql`) divides the paper's price by the item's **feet bought** (`unit_length`) when the item's unit is foot / feet / ft and feet bought is filled in; it leaves the price alone when the paper is already by the foot (closer to the cost we have than the divided one would be). `_add_cost_update` uses it, so every new paper is handled, and it adds "[roll $51.50 ÷ 250 ft]" to the line's description; Approvals shows that under the new cost in a small green box ("The paper says $51.50 for the roll. $51.50 ÷ 250 feet = $0.206 a foot", `.pbr-foot`). The line already waiting on this item (169) was changed to $0.206. **It only works when feet bought is filled in: on Nov 10 only this one of the 56 by-the-foot items had it.** The retired twin 677 ("Thermostat Wire 18/2 (75m/250ft roll)", sold each) still has its own $51.50 line (134): that one is right as a roll price.

## Price book: the search also finds retired items

- (Carlo, Nov 10: "I look up 14K09 and it can't find it but it should be able to".) 14K09 (item 5934, Lennox flue coupling; and 6288 "14K09 1") is in the price book but **retired**, and the page lists only items in use, so the search showed nothing. Now, when words are typed in the price book's search and the list is on "in use", the search also looks through the retired items (`loadPricebook` asks with `p_active` null, `withRet`): they come after the others, greyed and marked "retired", and a yellow note on the count line says "This one is retired… Open it to bring it back." (`PB.retFound`, `.pb-retnote`). With no search words the list is as before. Only the Price book page; the pickers on invoices, estimates and bookings still offer items in use only. The search does not look at suppliers' own part numbers (`supplier_items.supplier_sku`) yet.

## Price book: a retired part that is bought again comes back by itself

- (Carlo, Nov 10: "the system should bring any part back that has been retired but is purchased again".) `sync/extra/251_bring_back_retired.sql`, live Nov 10. `_pb_bring_back(item, why)` sets `active` and writes `audit_log` (action `update`, `new_values.why` = "Bought again: Lennox invoice …"). Called from two places: `_add_cost_update` (every part line on a supplier's paper goes through it, even when the cost is the same) and a trigger on `purchase_order_lines` (`po_line_bring_back`, a PO line with the part, PO not cancelled). **Only for a purchase dated in the last 90 days**, so loading old papers does not bring old things back. Hidden new items (`pending_new`) are left alone. No screen change: the part just shows in the price book again.
- 14K09 (item 5934) was brought back by hand the same day, at his word. Its name is only "14K09"; its twin 6288 ("14K09 1", Lennox 102862-02 Flue Coupling Service Kit, equipment, cost $0) was deleted for good the same day, at his word and after a yes to "this cannot be undone" (nothing used it: no invoice, estimate, PO or stock line).
- **Not done: the parts already retired that were bought in the last 60 days** (42 of the 5,879 retired on Nov 10, 14K09 among them). Some were retired on purpose because a newer twin replaced them (677, the thermostat wire roll, replaced by 5522), so I asked him before bringing them all back. The same twin problem holds going forward: if a paper names a retired twin's code, the twin comes back.

## Price book: labour hours on a part add to its sell price (Standard labour rate for parts)

- (Carlo, Nov 10, using 14K09: "when I add labor hours to this part it should only be used to increase the sell price of the part; if 1 hour of labor then add 120 dollars… have a place to set the Standard labor rate for parts".) **Rule price of a Part or Material = the markup rule's price for its cost + its Labour hours × the Standard labour rate for parts.** The hours are the item's own **Labour hours** box (`est_labour_hours`). The rate is one number for Parts (`service`) and Materials, `pricebook_settings.part_labour_rate`, **$120 an hour** to start. Equipment and Security are not touched (their hours are for booking lengths).
- **Where the rate is set: the Pricing page, in the Parts section and the Materials section (PR 6), in a dark blue framed box under the markup lines**: "Standard labour rate for parts $ [120.00] an hour" + **Save the labour rate** (`#prc-labrate`, `#prc-labsave`, RPC `set_part_labour_rate`, needs `pricebook.edit`, written in `audit_log`). I put it there because that page is where sell prices are decided; Settings → Pricing control has the labour **cost** per hour (what a tech costs us), which is a different number.
- Database: `sync/extra/252_part_labour_rate.sql` (live Nov 10): `_pb_labour_add(type, hours)`, `_pb_rule_price_item(type, cost, hours)`, and in-place patches (a DO block, each only once) so `pb_list` (the rule price and the "off the rule" list), `pb_apply_rule` (Set to the rule price), `pb_cost_updates` and `decide_cost_updates` (Approvals: rule price, and "approve and set prices by the markup rule") all add the labour; `pb_pricing` returns `part_labour_rate`. **No price was changed by the file.** `_pb_rule_price(type, cost)` itself is unchanged (markup only).
- App: `pbRuleBase` (markup only), `pbLabourAdd`, `pbRulePrice(type, cost, hours)`. The item's hint under Sell price says "The markup rule says $100.27 (markup $40.27 + 0.5 hours of labour × $120.00 = $60.00)" and changes as the hours are typed; **🔄 Update price** uses it and shows the same sum; the green box on the Pricing page adds "Then its labour is added: …". 14K09: cost $22.37 → markup $40.27 + 0.5 h = $100.27 (its price was $102.53; not changed, he presses Update price).
- **Watch out**: on Nov 10, 149 Parts and 211 Materials in use had labour hours, so the "off the markup" lists for Parts and Materials now show them with the labour in the rule price; "Set all to the rule price" would raise those. **Job costing: a Part's or Material's labour hours are not a labour cost any more** (Carlo, Nov 10, 6:15 p.m.: "yes stop counting them for jobs, just use their profit amount on the jobs, but have the labour we add on the part just show with the part mark up as one"). `sync/extra/253_part_labour_not_a_cost.sql` (live): in-place patches so `inv_costing` gives 0 hours for lines whose item is `service` or `material`, and `est_costing` (used by `estLineCost`: estimates, the invoice's expected cost, the item's own profit box) gives no `hours` and no own `lab` for them. So on a job a part's profit is one number: sell price (markup + labour add-on) less the part's cost. Equipment and Security keep their labour cost; parts inside a kit are left as they were (a kit's labour is the kit's). The new-item form's question 11 (labour hours × the labour cost rate, "total cost") still shows for Parts and Materials but no longer feeds job cost: reword or drop it there if he asks.

## Price book item: the friendlier page (four numbered sections, plain words)

- (Carlo, Nov 10, 7:45 p.m., after I listed nine ideas: "do it all".) The item's own box (`showPbItem`, used on the part's page and in the price book list's side panel) is now:

No steps for buttons, squares, the picture or headings. A box that is not on the page is skipped, so the count changes) and **🕘 What changed?** (`pbChanges`, `pbChangeWords`, `PB_CHG`, RPC `pb_item_changes`: the last 30 `audit_log` rows for the item as "Carlo changed the sell price from $102.53 to $100.27", newest first; cost changes only for people who see costs).
  - **Four sections, each with a number square, a plain heading and its own soft tone** (`.pb-sec`, `secH`; tone = `--st`, mixed into `var(--panel)` so it works in every colour theme): **1 What is it?** (blue: name, item number, kind, description, maker, model, Is it in use?) · **2 What does it cost and sell for?** (teal: sell price with its two buttons, what we pay, the waiting-cost banner, the plain money line, price for members, How is it sold?, hours of work, Charge tax?) · **3 How many do we have?** (sand: the Stock section) · **4 Who do we buy it from?** (violet: usual supplier, then the suppliers list). Under them, outside the form as before: "Which groups is it in?" (was Categories) and "Parts that always go with it" (was Add-on kit).
  - **The money in one sentence** under the price: "We pay $22.37. We sell it for $100.27. We make $77.90 each time we sell one." (`#pb-plain`, written by `showM`; "We lose…" when the price is under the cost). The margin / markup percents are only in the Accounting line now.
  - **Words changed**: Code (SKU) → Item number · Type → Kind · Our cost → What we pay · Member price → Price for members · Labour hours → Hours of work to put it in (with "adds to the sell price" on Parts and Materials) · Charge HST → "Charge tax (HST) on it?". The input names are the same (`sku`, `item_type`, `unit_cost`, `member_price`, `est_labour_hours`, `taxable`), so saving did not change. Older notes here that say "Labour hours box" mean "Hours of work to put it in".
  - **Yes / No squares** for "Is it in use?" and "Charge tax (HST) on it?" (`yn()`, `.pb-yn`, `.pb-yn-sq`; the chosen one is dark blue). The real tick box is still there, hidden, and a click sets it and fires `change`, so Save and the green "Save changes" bar work as before.
  - **Stock word and the order number**: beside the heading, "We have 46. Good." (green) / "We have 2. Time to order." (red) / "We have none. Time to order." / just "We have 46." when no number is set (`stWord`, `#pb-st-word`). The number is typed under the stock boxes: "Tell me to order when we have fewer than [ ]" + **Save the number** (`pricebook_items.reorder_below`, RPCs `pb_reorder`, `set_pb_reorder`; its own save, not the item's). It only shows a word on the item's page: no list of parts to order and no alert yet.
