## Trent Metal is two suppliers now: the main account and the water heater account (Nov 12)

- (Carlo, Nov 12, 11:15 a.m.: "Trent Metals has 2 accounts with us, a water heater account with customer number 4161 and a normal account with customer number 4112; I need you to separate any that have been combined and make it 2 accounts now".) Until now both were one supplier (id 8), with only the statements told apart by `supplier_statements.account`; the invoice list, the credit memos and Our statement mixed the two.
- **Now**: supplier **8 "Trent Metal Limited …" = the main account, 4112** (`account_number` filled in) and a new supplier **63 "Trent Metal Water Heaters (account 4161)"** (`sinvShort` shows "Trent Metal Water Heaters"; no ServiceTitan id, the sync does not touch it; the same logo). Done straight in the database (it is data, not in a `sync/extra` file).
- **What moved to 63**: the 14 statements of account 4161 and of 3748 (3748 is marked "old water heater account" in the statements, Dec 2021 to Sep 2022), the 281 invoices and credits whose first statement is one of those ($50,868.01 in all), their 99 Credit Memos, and Vista's 17 tank payments to Trent (`third_party_supplier_payments`). Left on 8: 43 statements (4112), 450 invoices and credits ($173,352.87), 73 Credit Memos, all 604 POs, the 663 supplier part numbers and the 207 "usual supplier" items (none of those say which account they are for).
- **How each invoice was placed**: by the account of the statement it was first billed on (`supplier_invoices.statement_id`). Every invoice but three had one.
- **The one that was truly combined**: credit "UnappliedCR" (invoice 3594, −$111.12) was one record tied to lines on both accounts' statements (the main account's own UnappliedCR of −$2,897.28 on Sep and Oct 2025 had been tied to it by its number). It went to the water heater account, where its amount is; the two main-account lines are untied (they show as lines with no invoice of ours).
- **Not placed, waiting on Carlo**: three invoices on no statement yet, left on the main account: S1599933-001 (Oct 29, $1,494.74), S1599951-001 (Oct 29, $851.76), S1601753-001 (Nov 2, $4,611.25). Their pages are scans with no saved words, so I could not read which account. He looks at the Bill To; to move one: `update supplier_invoices set supplier_id = 63 where id = …`.
- **How to tell the accounts on a paper**: Trent prints **"WATER HEATER ACCOUNT ONLY"** under our name in Bill To / Sold To on the water heater account's papers (seen in the emailed papers' words); the main account's do not have it.
- **Partly built Nov 12 (see "Trent Metal (TML Supply) statements are read now")**: the statement loader and drop-in now pick the account by "WATER HEATER ACCOUNT"; the mailbox reader does not yet. What follows is how it was: A Trent paper from the mailbox is still guessed as "Trent" (the first supplier whose name starts with it, id 8), and a 4161 statement dropped on the main account's page would be saved there with account 4161. Until it is built, the person picks "Trent Metal Water Heaters" in step 1 of the paper, or drops it on that supplier's page. To build: when the words have "WATER HEATER ACCOUNT", use supplier 63 (mail reader, drop-in and the statement loader).
- **To undo**: table `supplier_split_log` lists every row that moved (what, row id, from, to). Put each back to supplier 8, set invoice 3594's `statement_id` back to 149 and tie the two lines back, then delete supplier 63 and its logo.
- Checked after: no statement line of one supplier is tied to an invoice of the other, and no invoice sits on the other supplier's statement. **Not looked at in the app.**

## Trent Metal (TML Supply) statements are read now (Nov 12)

- (Carlo, Nov 12, 1:04 p.m., with doc07785620261112122256.pdf: "system can read this"; I took it as "can not". The file is 34 pages: the water heater account's statement of Mar 31, 2026, 2 pages, then 32 ship tickets.) **Cause: `stParse` had no pattern for Trent's layout**, so it found no lines and took the first line's date (Nov 27, 2025) for the statement's. Trent prints as "TML Supply Company"; a line is `03/02/26 S1525441.001 28191335 1744.72 1744.72` (date, invoice number with its .001, our PO, invoice amount, payments / credits, net due). The reader gives the S as `$`, and pen marks sit between the number and the PO.
- **Now** (`ST_TML_LINE`, `stTmlLines`, the `TM` block at the top of the line loop in `stParse`): each line is kept as `S1525441.001` with the net due (the last amount; negative when any amount on the line has a minus, because the minus is missed on one of the two readings of a credit), code I, or C for a credit (the `.002` lines), and its PO. "Unapplied CR" lines are kept as `UnappliedCR-120325` (the date in the name, so two on one statement are two lines, and they are not tied by number to the old "UnappliedCR" invoice). The date and the account come from the top boxes ("4161 03/31/26"), else the end of the newest line's month. The total is the NEW BALANCE: the last box of the row that adds up. `R.account` fills the statement's account when the page has none.
- **Only the statement's own pages are read** (`stReadFiles`): each Trent statement page but the last says "Continued on Next Page", so reading stops after the first page without it; the ship tickets after it are not read (about 20 seconds instead of 6 minutes), and the usual line says to drop the file on Supplier invoices for those.
- **The page's Trent account is picked by itself** (`sinvTrentSup`, first thing in `sinvWhichSup`): a Trent / TML page with "WATER HEATER ACCOUNT" goes to Trent Metal Water Heaters, one without it to the main account, whichever page it was dropped on. Drop-in (`kindOf`) also calls a page with three such lines a statement. **The mailbox reader (`sync/supplier_mail.py`) was not changed**: an emailed Trent paper is still guessed as the main account.
- Checked: the script parses; his file's two statement pages were read with the same kind of reader (tesseract on this machine, not the browser's copy) and run through the app's real `stParse`: 37 lines, Mar 31, 2026, account 4161, and the lines add up to $22,454.63, the statement's own New Balance. **The loader in the browser, the page stop, the account pick and Save were not run, and nothing was seen on the live app. The statement was not saved by me.** None of its March invoices are in the app yet (the ship tickets are not invoices): after saving, the lines will say "Invoice not loaded in our system" with "Make our invoice from their statement".

- **Drag and Drop: Trent's ship tickets and credit memos get their number now** (Carlo, Nov 12, 1:15 p.m., with a picture of the drop-in list: "Trent still not working, missed about 10 of them"; the rows said "Type the invoice number in the box"). He had dropped the same 34-page file on Drag and Drop, not Load a statement. `numOf` in `dropInGroups` had no pattern for Trent's number, so a page only got one when it happened to match a statement line by its digits. Now `numOf` reads "S1533807.001" (the `$` or `5` the reader gives for the S is put right) on a Trent page. `kindOf`: a Trent page with SOLD TO / SHIP FROM / Picked by / Customer Signature and no BILL TO / REMIT TO / CREDIT MEMO is a **slip** (the words "Ship Ticket" are often not read), so **ship tickets are loaded as packing slips** (that is what the new "Need to find the packing slip" line looks for); Trent's invoices and credit memos say BILL TO / REMIT TO and stay invoices. A Trent statement's last page with one line is still a statement page (it was taken for an invoice with that line's number). Checked: all 34 pages were read here (tesseract on this machine, not the browser's) and run through the app's real `dropInGroups`: the 2-page statement, 14 ship tickets and 6 credit memos, each with its own number, none missing. **How each is then saved (credit or invoice, the amounts, the P.O.) was not run, and nothing was seen on the live app.**

## Supplier invoices page: bigger squares, all the same size (Nov 12)

- (Carlo, Nov 12: "on the supplier page make all the buttons 25% bigger or more if needed so they fill all the text in them and we can see it all, but keep them all the same size… we want this to be symmetric".) One CSS block at the end of the main style sheet, only for `body[data-page="supplier-invoices"]` (the other pages that reuse `.sinv-app` are not changed). **The supplier squares (`.sinv-tabs button`) and the statement squares (`.sinv-app`: All invoices, By year, Load, Drag and Drop, Make a payment, Our statement…, the years, the months) are 164px (they were 124px, about a third bigger), words 15px (were 12.5px), pictures 64px.** A supplier's name is no longer cut at two lines (it was `-webkit-line-clamp: 2`; that is what hid "Trent Metal Water Heaters"). The squares are not forced square any more (`aspect-ratio: auto`, `min-height: 164px`) and each grid has `grid-auto-rows: 1fr`, so if one square ever needs to be taller for its words, every square in that group gets taller with it: never two sizes in one group. **The app boxes at the top of an open supplier invoice (`.apb.sci-big`) are 240px by 204px (were 190 by 150 and of uneven heights)**, in a grid with `subgrid`, so the boxes are one height even when one has "Wrong PO? Take it off" under it. **The app boxes of an open invoice are always in one row** (Carlo, Nov 12, 4:59 p.m., with a picture where Our Stock had dropped to a second line: "I want all 4 boxes always in a row"): `.sci-apps` lays its boxes out in one row whatever their number (`grid-auto-flow: column`, each up to 240px) and when there is less room they all get narrower together, words too (`container-type: inline-size`, sizes in `cqw` inside `clamp()`): 197px each in the 830px he has, 133px at 560px. Looked at in a test picture at 560, 830 and 1300px. Not seen on the live app. **The app boxes of an open invoice are soft and light** (Carlo, Nov 12, 5:04 p.m.: "soften up the 4 boxes from the supplier invoices, make them look friendlier"): white fading to a very pale blue, a thin pale blue edge, dark blue words (#23415f), a faint shadow, 12px corners, the picture on a pale blue square, and the small name on top in normal letters, not capitals ("Supplier P.O."). A box that is found or done (`apb-green`) is the same in pale green with dark green words. They used to be solid dark blue and solid green with white words and a heavy shadow. CSS only, one block under the one-row rule, only `.apb.sci-big` on this page; sizes and clicks are as before. Looked at in a test picture. Not seen on the live app. **Owners can move the app boxes of an open invoice: hold one, then drag it** (Carlo, Nov 12, 5:21 p.m.: "also make it so I can move the buttons around"). Hold the mouse (or a finger) on a box for about a third of a second: it gets a yellow frame; drag it onto another box and it takes that place; let go and the order is saved **for everyone**, on every supplier invoice ("✓ The new order is saved for everyone."). Only owners can move them (the same as the Reason for Call buttons); a short click still opens the box, and the click that ends a drag does not. The order is kept by kind of box: `po`, `memo`, `inv` (all the Customer Invoice boxes move together), `slip`, `stock` (`SCI_KINDS`, `SCI_RO`, `sciOrder`, `sciBoxes`, `sciBoxDrop`; each `.sci-app` carries `data-sci-kind`); a box that is not on the invoice being looked at keeps its place after the ones that are. Kept in `company_settings.sci_box_order` (RPCs `sci_box_order()`, `set_sci_box_order(p)` owner only; `sync/extra/265_sci_box_order.sql`, live Nov 12) and in localStorage `reid.sci.order`. Checked in a test browser with the real code (hold, drag the last box to the front, saved order, no click from the drag, a click after still works). Not seen on the live app, and not tried with a finger on a phone. I left the corners as they were. Checked in a test picture with the app's styles and long names at 1150px and 760px: one size in each group, no words cut. **Not seen on the live app. All suppliers / Click to add a new supplier sit in their own column, so if a supplier name ever runs to four lines that row would be taller than those two.**

## Supplier invoices: a supplier opens on its newest statement's month, in green (Nov 12)

- (Carlo, Nov 12, 5:43 p.m.: "when I pick a supplier have it auto open to By year, current year, and scroll down to the current month and have the current month in green; that would be the one that has the last supplier statement loaded up".) A click on a supplier square now opens **By year**, on the **year of that supplier's newest statement**, with **that statement's month picked** (so its statement is drawn in the page), and the page scrolls to that month square (`SINV.toMonth`, done in `wireSupInv`; the scroll is done three times in the first second because the statement under it is still loading). **The month square of the newest statement is green** (`.sa-latest`, #1f9d4a with white words; a yellow ring when it is also the picked one), whenever that year's months show. "Current" is the newest statement loaded, as he said, not today's calendar month: for a supplier whose last statement is from an older year, that year opens. **Fixed at 5:46 p.m.** ("when I click the supplier it's not auto scrolling and the green you picked is too dark, make it lighter"): the scroll was being undone by the app-wide "Nothing moves" rule, which holds a clicked square (anything with `aria-pressed`) at the same spot on the screen for up to 8 seconds; supplier squares (`[data-sinv-sup]`) are now left out of that rule, and the scroll is done five times in the first 1.6 seconds. The green is a light green now (#bfe8cb, a mid green edge, dark green words), not #1f9d4a with white words. **Lesson: anything that scrolls the page by itself after a click on an `aria-pressed` square must be left out of the "Nothing moves" rule.** **A click on the supplier that is already picked does it again** (Carlo, 5:49 p.m., with a picture of Boone already highlighted after a reload: "if I click it it should bring me down; it does not if it's highlighted"): the click used to do nothing when the supplier was already the picked one; now it goes to By year, the newest statement's month, and scrolls there, the same as a first click. **The page goes down to the statement itself, not the month square** (Carlo, Nov 12, 7:44 p.m.: "don't bring me to the month, bring me even lower to the statement"): the top of the statement drawn in the page (`#stv-inline`) is put just under the top bar, again a few times in the first two and a half seconds while it loads, and it stops when the person scrolls or clicks. The newest month's square is still green, now above the screen. A supplier with no statement yet opens on all of its invoices as before. All invoices and the other years and months are one click away as before. Where older notes say "a supplier tab opens on all of its invoices", read this. Checked: the script parses. **Not run, not looked at in a picture and not seen on the live app.**

## Supplier invoices: the month's statement is in the page, not a pop-up (Nov 12)

- (Carlo, Nov 12, 11:36 a.m., minutes after the note above: "when I click Aug I want it to appear below and stay there unless I click something else, like the details used to do; I do not want it to be a pop up".) **Where the note above says a month click opens the statement pop-up, read this.** A click on a month square only picks the month; the page then draws that month's statement **in the page, under the squares and the statement's cards**, where the table used to be (`<section id="stv-inline" data-st>` in `renderSupInv` when `stIn`; `wireSupInv` calls `openSupStatement(id, sup, true)`; `STV.inline`, and `stvPaper()` = the inline box or `#inv-paper`, used all through `openSupStatement` / `drawSupStatement`). It stays until another month, year, supplier or All invoices is clicked. When the page is only drawn again it shows what it has with no reload (`STV.src === SINV.data`). A click on an invoice in it opens that invoice's page; **‹ Back to the statement** and ‹ Back to the list both come back to the page with the statement in it (the back handler sets `SINV.st` and clears `SINV.backTo`; it no longer opens the pop-up). The big "📄 Open the August 2026 statement" button is gone (the statement is right there); the small "$… 📄" total in the statement's cards still opens the pop-up page, which is the one that prints. **A month under "Not on a statement yet" is back to how it was before today**: its table, and the "Open the … statement so far" button for the pop-up (Our statement was not moved into the page). Loading a new statement (`openNewStatement`) still uses the pop-up. Checked: the script parses. **Nothing was run or looked at in a picture, and it was not seen on the live app.**

- **A month with no statement yet looks like a statement too** (Carlo, Nov 12, 1:55 p.m., with pictures of October for Trent Metal Water Heaters as a table and September as a statement: "when there is no statement yet… still list it like the ones that have a statement"). Where the note above says a month under "Not on a statement yet" keeps its table, read this. A picked pending month (`pendIn` in `renderSupInv`) hides the filter bar and the table (`sinv-monthpop`) and draws `sinvPendStmt(rows, D)` in the page: the same paper (`.stv-inline > .stv`): logo and name, a box "STATEMENT SO FAR" with the date (end of the month, or today) and the customer number (from the supplier's last statement), then Date · Reference · Br. · CD · Amount · Balance due · Our records, one line for each of our invoices and credits of that month (amount with tax, credits with the minus behind like theirs; CD is I, C or D), and TOTAL SO FAR. No aging boxes (the cards above it have them). A click anywhere on a line opens that invoice (`tr.stv-go[data-sinv]`, the list's own click, so ‹ Back to the list comes back to the same spot). The "Open the … statement so far" button (Our statement, the pop-up that prints, with what is carried from before) is still above it. Checked: the script parses. **Not run, not looked at in a picture and not seen on the live app.**

- **A last column on the statement: Supplier invoice · On file or Missing** (Carlo, Nov 12, 2:01 p.m.: "add another column at the end stating Supplier invoice On file or Missing"). `stvFileCell(r)`: **On file** (green) when our record of that line has the supplier's own paper saved (`pages` over 0); **Missing** (red) when it has no copy, or when we have no record of the line at all. On the month's statement, its pop-up copy, and the "statement so far" of a month with no statement yet. I took "on file" to mean we hold a copy of their paper, not only a record made from a statement. Checked: the script parses. **Not looked at in a picture and not seen on the live app.**

- **A P.O. column, last on the statement** (Carlo, Nov 12, 2:08 p.m.: "add a column that is for the P.O.'s only so they match or they are over or they are under or there is none"). `stvPoCell(r)`: **Match** (green; within $1) · **Over by $…** / **Under by $…** (amber; the invoice before tax against the P.O., the same numbers as `sinvState`) · **None** (red; no P.O. of ours on it, or no record of the line) · "No amounts on it" when the P.O. has no prices; the P.O. number is small under the word. A credit, return or charge shows only its P.O. number (not compared) or None. **So nothing is said twice, "Our records" no longer has the P.O. words or number** (`stvOurWords`): it reads "In our system", "✓ Checked", "Credit", "Return ticket", "Service charge", the not-loaded line, or "Invoice not loaded in our system". Columns now: Date · Reference · Br. · CD · Amount · Balance due · Our records · Supplier invoice · P.O. On the month's statement, its pop-up copy and the statement so far. Checked: the script parses. **Not looked at in a picture and not seen on the live app; nine columns may need sideways scrolling on a narrow screen.**

- **"Our records" is gone: six columns, one question each** (Carlo, Nov 12, 2:10 p.m.: "I want to break it up into columns so each column tells you what to do or not do"; 2:14 p.m.: "don't worry about the width, just do it"). The statement's columns are now Date · Reference · Br. · CD · Amount · Balance due, then (`STV_HEAD`, `stvCols(r, diff)`): **In our system** (Yes / No) · **Supplier invoice** (On file / Missing) · **P.O.** (Match / Over by / Under by / None) · **Customer invoice or stock** (Found with its number / In our stock / Needed) · **Packing slip** (Found with its number / This paper is it / Missing) · **Checked** (Yes / Not yet). **Green = nothing to do, red or amber = it needs you** (red is `.st-no` inside `.stv-c` / `.stv-po`). A credit, return or charge says "Not needed" in the customer invoice and packing slip columns; a line with no record of ours says No, Missing, None and dashes. Where the notes above say "Our records", read this (`stvOurWords` is gone; the red "differs from the paper" line of a compare is under In our system). The customer invoice, stock and packing slip answers come from one new look-up for all the lines, RPC **`sinv_line_flags(p_ids)`** (`sync/extra/263_sinv_line_flags.sql`, live Nov 12; people who see costs; about 0.3 s for 40 lines: it reads every packing slip once, since a look-up for each line took 16 s), kept in `STV_FLAGS`; those two columns show "…" for a moment, then the statement is drawn again (`stvFlagsLoad`). An invoice opened and changed (an invoice attached, put in stock) shows the old answer on the statement until the app is refreshed (`STV_FLAGS` is not cleared). The packing slip rule is the same as the open invoice's box: another paper marked packing slip, with a copy, on the same P.O., the same printed P.O. or the same number. On the month's statement, its pop-up copy and the statement so far. Checked: the script parses; the function was timed on the live database; the columns were drawn from the real code with pretend lines and looked at in a picture at 1150px (they fit). **The look-up from the app and the redraw were not run, and it was not seen on the live app.**

- **A sideways scroll bar when the statement does not fit the screen** (Carlo, Nov 12, 2:23 p.m.: "add a scroll bar to it for smaller screens and have it pop up whenever it does not fit the screen"). `stvScrollWrap(root)` (called in `drawSupStatement` and at the top of `wireSupInv`) puts the statement's table in `.stv-scroll` and adds a second bar above it (`.stv-topbar`, kept in step with the one under it) so the bar can be reached without going to the bottom of a long statement, with one line, "This list is wider than your screen. Slide the grey bar sideways to see the rest. →" (`.stv-scrollhint`). **The bars and the line show only when the table is wider than its box** (`stvScrollFit`, checked again when the window changes size); the bar is thick and grey (16px) so it is easy to grab. The table first shrinks as far as its words allow (headings wrap), and only then scrolls. The statement drawn in the page can be up to 1240px wide now (`.stv` was held to 960px, less than the twelve columns need, so it would have scrolled on every screen). Not printed. Checked in a test browser at 700px (bars and line show, the two bars move together) and 1300px (no bar). **Not seen on the live app.**

- **A search box over the statement's lines** (Carlo, Nov 12, 7:54 p.m., with a picture of the statement in the page: "add a search bar to the area shown"). Right above the list, under the company name: "🔎 Search this statement" with a big box (`.stv-find`, made in `stvScrollWrap`, so it is on the month's statement, its pop-up copy and the statement so far). Type an invoice number, a P.O., an amount (with or without the comma) or a word from the columns (Missing, Needed, Under, Match…): only the lines with every word typed stay, and it says "Showing 3 of 42 lines" with ✕ Show all. Nothing is loaded again; lines are hidden in place. The words stay when the statement is drawn again (`STV.findQ`) and start empty on another statement. Not printed. Checked: the script parses. **Not run, not looked at in a picture and not seen on the live app.**
- **A credit line on the statement looks for its credit paper and its Credit Memo** (Carlo, Nov 12, 8:00 p.m.: "any line that shows a negative or credit under the amount column should look for a credit invoice or credit memo to match it"). Two things. **(1) Finding our credit paper**: a line with a minus amount or code C that has no record of ours under its number is now matched to one of our credit papers of that supplier **by its amount** (the same amount to the cent, dated from 4 months before the statement to 5 days after, not used by another line of this statement, and only when exactly one fits; `drawSupStatement`). It then reads Yes under In our system with "Credit C373025, matched by its amount" and the line opens that credit. It is only how the statement is drawn: nothing is saved, and a credit already tied to another month's statement is not ruled out. **(2) The Credit Memo**: for a credit (or return) line, the "Customer invoice or stock" column no longer says "Not needed": it says green **Credit Memo** with its number (from `SINV.memos`), or red **Credit Memo needed**; open the credit and press the blue Create a Credit Memo box. Packing slip still says Not needed. A credit line with no paper of ours at all says No / Missing and can be clicked to load it or make one from the system (see the Missing note). On the month's statement, its pop-up copy and the statement so far. Checked: the script parses. **Not run, not looked at in a picture and not seen on the live app.**
- **Date is the first column of the month's statement** (Carlo, Nov 12, 11:43 a.m.: "section SU 17, make the Date the first column"). The statement's table (`table.stv-t` in `drawSupStatement`) now reads **Date · Reference · Br. · CD · Amount · Balance due · Our records** (it was Reference · Br. · Date · CD…). I took SU 17 to be the statement drawn in the page; I could not see his page to check the number. The pop-up copy of the statement (the one that prints) uses the same table, so it changed too. The supplier's own paper has Reference first, so ours no longer matches theirs column for column. The "check what was read" table when a new statement is loaded (`stReviewHtml`) was not changed. Checked: the script parses. **Not seen on the live app.**

- **A click anywhere on a line of the statement opens its invoice** (Carlo, Nov 12, 11:46 a.m.: "when I click anywhere on any line have it open up, not just on the reference"). Each line that has an invoice of ours is `tr.stv-go` (a hand for the mouse, a light blue wash under it, Enter works too); the click presses the line's own reference button (`data-stv-inv`), so it opens the same way. A click while words are selected does nothing (so text can still be copied). **A line with no invoice of ours ("Invoice not loaded in our system") has nothing to open and is not clickable.** Same table in the pop-up copy. Checked: the script parses. **The click was not run and it was not seen on the live app.**

## Supplier invoices: an open invoice says what it still needs, and Back goes to the same line (Nov 12)

- (Carlo, Nov 12, about noon, with invoice S1551413.001: "it should say Found the P.O. But its off by and the amount. it should say need to find the customer invoice or put it in our stock still. Need to find the packing slip".) At the top of an open supplier invoice, above the app boxes, a white box with an amber left edge: **"This invoice still needs 3 things"** and one plain line for each (`sinvNeedsHtml(r, d)`, `.sinv-needs`, called first in `sinvCustHtml`; green edge and "This invoice has everything" when nothing is left). Invoices only (not credits, returns, charges). The three lines:
  - **P.O.**: "Need to find the P.O." · "P.O. … is printed on it, but that P.O. is not in our system." · "Found the P.O. …. It matches." · "Found the P.O. …. But it is off by **$212.92**. The invoice is $1,406.25 before tax. The P.O. is $1,193.33." · "…But the P.O. has no amounts on it, so I can not compare." (the same numbers as `sinvState`; within $1 is a match).

Under the square, for people who can edit, "✕ Take it off" (`data-sci-off`). The suggestions ("On this PO's job") and Find our invoice results are still lines with ＋ Attach. `sinvCustHtml`, `.sci-apps` in `index.html`.

Found and Matched" with the number (a click opens the PO). **Customer Invoice** (I took his "Supplier invoice" box to mean our invoice for the parts, since the supplier's invoice is the page that is open): blue "Click to find the invoice" (a click shows Find our invoice and the "On this PO's job" suggestions, `SINV.invFind`) or green "Invoice Found and Matched" with its number (a click opens it), one per attached invoice, plus "＋ Attach another invoice". Read it again and Print the invoice are on their own line under the boxes (`sinvToolsHtml`). `sinvPoTile`, `sinvPoFindHtml`, `sinvCustHtml`, `.sci-big` in `index.html`. This replaces the layout described in the notes just above and below (square "Invoice attached", "PO found", Match to PO always showing).

Dark blue, bold, small; they are gone as soon as an invoice is found or the parts are in stock (then the green and grey boxes say it). Not printed. Looked at in a test picture at 830px and 560px. Not seen on the live app. "Not a packing slip? Undo" still shows only when a person marked it; a paper the reader took for a packing slip has no undo yet. **The document also stays on the supplier's invoice list** (Our statement needs it there); if Carlo wants it gone from that list, that is a separate change. Not offered on credits and charges. `sinvSlipTile`, `data-sci-slip` in `index.html`.
- **A fourth app box: Our Stock** (Carlo, Nov 10: "add a fourth button called add it to our stock and if it can not find the parts then have a note saying it can not add the parts, check the invoice again"). Blue "Add it to our stock": a click opens the stock line under the boxes (`SINV.stockOpen`; it is hidden until then): the place to put the parts and the 📦 Put these parts in stock button (it still asks first), or, when none of the parts are in our price list, the red note **"I can not add the parts. Check the invoice again."** with the parts it could not find. Green "Added to our stock" once done (a click shows what went in, where, when and who). **The stock line that opens under the boxes stands out a little more** (Carlo, Nov 12, 5:44 p.m.: "more obvious without changing the colour very much, maybe a darker border or some drop shadow"): it is a box now, with a 2px dark blue edge (#23415f), a soft shadow and a very light wash (`.sci-stock` on this page); it used to be only a thin grey line above the words. Not looked at in a picture, not seen on the live app. The rules under "Supplier invoices: Add to our stock" below still hold. `sinvStockTile`, `data-sci-stockopen`, `sinvStockHtml`.
- **Find our invoice also looks by amount** (Carlo, Nov 12, 8:12 p.m., on credit C438842: "have the Find our invoice also search by amount so you can search by the credit amount to see what it may match"). Type an amount with its cents in the Find our invoice box (691.56, $1,654.32, with or without a minus): it lists our customer invoices whose total or before-tax amount is that amount, or that amount with or without 13% HST added (up to 20, an exact total first). Anything else typed searches as before (a number with no cents is still an invoice or job number). Under the box: "Or look by this credit's amount: **$691.56 with tax** · **$612.00 before tax**" — one click types it and searches (`data-sci-amt`, `.sci-amts`). RPC `sinv_cust_find` (`sync/extra/269_sinv_cust_find_amount.sql`, live Nov 12). Also used by the other place that calls `sinv_cust_find`. Checked: the script parses; the amount test was run on the live database (3 of our invoices fit $691.56 one way or another). **The box itself was not run and it was not seen on the live app. It only looks at our customer invoices, not at the supplier's own invoices.**
- **Boone "Sales order / Packing slip" (C440377) is read now too** (Carlo, Nov 10: "it could not read the po or anything on this one"). One-line layout with no net amount, only the 5-decimal price; a kit's COMPONENT lines have no price and are left out, the kit line carries it (KITNIP05B 12 × $13.56 = $162.72, which is the PO's "1/2 Nipple Kit" line). Third fallback pattern in `sinvParseLines`. Its PO sits on a shaded band the plain reading misses, so **Read it again**, when the invoice has no PO and none shows in the plain reading, looks again with `smScanAgain` (the Auto update reader: shading washed out, loose words; about half a minute a page), then keeps a number only if it is a PO in the app (`po_list`, this supplier first). If the PO is found but no lines, it offers to match the PO alone. Tested with the app's reader on his file: the line is picked out and 116501859 comes back in "[numbers read again]"; that PO is in the app for Boone. The part number can come back with look-alike letters (KITNIPOSB for KITNIP05B): scans do that.

## Supplier invoices: Add to our stock

- (Carlo, Nov 10.) In an open supplier invoice, in the "🧾 Customer invoice" section, under **Find our invoice**, there is a line **Add to our stock**: pick the place (warehouses and other non-truck places, Main warehouse first) and press **📦 Put these parts in stock**; it asks "Put these 3 parts in Main warehouse?" first. Each invoice line (code + shipped qty) is matched to our price list by that supplier's own part number (`supplier_items.supplier_sku`), else by our code (`sku`); matched parts go in with `stock_move` (kind in, note "From supplier invoice … (supplier)"), so they show in Stock history and can be undone there. Parts not in the price list are NOT added and are listed ("Not in our price list, so not added: …"). An invoice can only be added once (table `supplier_invoice_stock`; if every move was undone it can be added again); after, the line shows "📦 Added to our stock · place · day · who" and the parts. Credits, returns and charges do not show the line. RPC `supplier_invoice_to_stock(p_id, p_place, p_dry)` (dry = just look), `sync/extra/242_supplier_invoice_to_stock.sql`; `sinvStockHtml`, `d.stock`, `SINV.places` in `index.html`.

## Supplier invoices: Match to PO at the top

- (Carlo, Nov 10.) **Match to PO** is in the top section of an open supplier invoice, right under **Find our invoice** (it used to be lower, with Note / Date / Mark checked, which stay where they were). **Once the invoice has a PO, that spot says "✔ PO found: PO …" and there is no box to search again.** Only when the PO was matched by hand it also shows "Wrong PO? Take it off" (the old "Use the printed PO": it clears the hand match), so a mistake can be fixed. `sinvPoLineHtml`, `.sci-po` in `index.html`; same `data-sinv-setpo` / `data-sinv-unpo` handlers as before.

- **Match to PO searches every PO as you type** (Carlo, Nov 10: "should search all the P.O.s and start to match them as soon as you type a number"). From 2 characters, up to 8 POs that match show under the box (`po_list` search: PO number, job, customer; number matches first, then this supplier's, newest first; cancelled ones left out); click one and it is matched. Enter matches the only one showing, or the number as typed. The Match button still works. `data-sci-pofound`, `data-sci-popick` in `renderSupInv`; same idea as "Attach a PO by number" on Auto update.

- The **Note / Save note** box on an open supplier invoice was taken off (Carlo, Nov 10: "not sure what good it is"). Notes saved before are still in the database (`match_note`) and still show where the lists showed them; there is just no box to type one.

- **Clicking an invoice with no PO opens only the invoice** (Carlo, Nov 10: clicking C443072 "pops up with a print page… remove the print pop up, the normal one is good enough"). The big "New PO for …" page (`openMkPoPop`, shown in the invoice viewer with its Print bar) used to open by itself the first time such an invoice was clicked; that automatic open is gone. The make-a-PO box inside the open invoice (`data-mkpo`, its `data-mkpo-big` button) still opens it by hand.

## Supplier invoices: Read it again

- (Carlo, Nov 10: "add a button to re read the invoice in case it was read wrong the first time".) An open supplier invoice that has scanned pages shows **🔄 Read it again** at the top, on the Match to PO line, right beside the PO box and Match button, or beside "PO found" (Carlo, Nov 10: "move the read it again to the top next to Match PO box"; `sinvPoLineHtml(r, edit, d)`). It reads the saved pages again in the browser (tesseract, plus `stReadBoxes` for ruled forms; about 15 seconds a page), picks out the lines (`sinvParseLines`, `sinvDedupLines`), then shows the new reading beside what the invoice has now (lines and totals, and the invoice's before-tax amount) and asks "Use the new reading?". Only a yes saves it: RPC `supplier_invoice_reread(p_id, p_lines, p_extra)` replaces the read lines, keeps lines typed in by hand (`by_hand`), adds "Read again in the app on …" to `read_notes` and puts the old lines in `audit_log`. No lines found = nothing changes. It does not change the PO, the date or the totals. `sync/extra/243_supplier_invoice_reread.sql`; `data-sinv-reread` in `renderSupInv`. An invoice with no copy has no button (use ⬆ Add the invoice).

- **While Read it again is working, Eco Man pops up in the middle of the screen, thinking** (Carlo, Nov 10: "have the eco man pop up and look like he is thinking until it's done reading"): the confused Eco Man picture (the moving one) rocking a little under three thought dots, "Hmm, let me think…" and under it what he is doing right now ("Reading page 1 of 2…", "Looking for the PO number…"). He goes away the moment the reading is done, before the "Use the new reading?" question. `ecoThink(words)` → `{ say, close }`, `.eco-think` in `index.html`; use it for other slow jobs too.

- **🖨 Print the invoice** is beside Read it again on the Match to PO line (Carlo, Nov 10). It opens a new window with the invoice's scanned pages, one to a sheet, and the print box opens by itself (`data-sinv-print` in `renderSupInv`). Only for invoices that have a copy. If the browser blocks the window it says to allow pop-ups.

- **Boone pick tickets ("Sales order / Pick ticket", like C443072) are read now** (Carlo, Nov 10: he printed C443072 and said the system can not read it; it was saved with no lines, no PO and $0). The page words were read fine; the line picker did not know this layout: the line number and part number sit on one line ("001 |LF4525050") and the words, ordered / shipped / back-ordered, the 5-decimal price, the discount and the net amount on the next one or two. `sinvParseLines` now falls back to that two-line pattern when the usual one finds nothing (so new pick tickets dropped in get their lines too), and `sinvPoFromText` reads the PO from under "Customer PO#". **Read it again** also passes that PO (`p_extra.po_text`): it is kept only when the invoice has no PO, and matched if that PO is in the app. Tested on his file with the app's own reader: 2 lines ($11.94 + $21.84 = $33.78) and PO 116611689. Read it again does not change the dollar total (a pick ticket stays $0 until Boone's invoice or statement gives the amount: see the $0 packing slip rule under Our statement).

- **"Under the PO by $1,803.00" when every line matched** (Carlo, Nov 10, Boone C440593). A pick ticket is saved with $0 and no subtotal, so the list compared $0 with the PO, while the line-by-line compare inside (which already added up the lines) showed a match. Now `supplier_invoices_list` also returns `lines_amount` (the invoice's own lines added up, `_sinv_lines_amount`, `sync/extra/244_sinv_lines_amount.sql`: it patches the list function in place) and `sinvBefore` uses it when the invoice has no subtotal and a $0 total. So such an invoice says "Matches PO" when its lines add up to the PO. 292 invoices were in this spot on Nov 10. (The column is also written straight into `sync/extra/059_supplier_accounts.sql`, the file that defines the list, with the helper at its top: with only the 244 patch, the column was missing for the minutes between 059 and 244 on every database update, and Carlo saw "Under the PO" come back during one.) The saved total is not changed (still $0 on Our statement until their invoice or statement gives the amount).

## Supplier invoices: put a PO line back

- (Carlo, Nov 10.) In an open supplier invoice, "Changes to this PO" shows **↩ Put it back** beside each "Took off" line. It asks first, then adds the line again with the same words, quantity and price (the usual PO save, reason "Put back: it was taken off by mistake"), and the entry then says "(put back)". `sinvHistory`, `data-sinv-putback` in `index.html`; no database change. It comes back as a new line, so anything tied to the old line (received counts) is not carried over.

## Our statement (suppliers): invoices that miss their statement

- **"👁 View the document" on Check what was read** (Carlo, Nov 10): beside Save the statement in the statement loader (`stReviewHtml`); it opens the statement's own pages big over the screen (`stViewDoc`, `#stv-doc`, from `STV.read.imgs`), Close or Esc shuts it, so the lines can be checked against the paper before saving.
- **Heating Parts Central (HPC, supplier 26) statements**: one page, "Statement" in a heavy box top right, date as month/day/year (8/10/2026 = Aug 10), lines like "6/8/2026 SI 342262 103384404 SALES INVOICE 7/8/2026 253.52 0.00 253.52" (`hp` in `stParse`, reference `SI-342262`), aging boxes Current · 1-30 · 31-60 · 61-90 · 91+ Days · Balance Due, often with the Work Order (SO-…) as page 2. On Nov 10 Carlo could not load one "as a statement" (doc07775620261110091353.pdf, Aug 10, one line, $253.52). **The real cause (found by running the app's own reader on his file): the browser reader skipped the whole ruled, shaded table**, so the word "Statement", the invoice line and the aging boxes never came back and page 1 was taken for an invoice. Fix: `stReadBoxes` (used by `dropInFiles` and `stReadFiles`) reads a page a second time with the form's lines wiped (`smNoLines`, one block) when fewer than 4 amounts were read, and adds those words under the first reading. With that, the file comes out as a statement dated Aug 10, 2026 with line SI-342262 $253.52, and page 2 as the work order SO-336570. (`kindOf` also calls a page a statement when it has three of the aging boxes and "Balance Due" or "Credit Limit".) To test the reader on a file without the live app: scratch `ocr/run.js` pattern = Playwright + the repo's index.html served locally, CDN scripts routed to `npm i tesseract.js@5.1.1 pdfjs-dist@3.11.174 @tesseract.js-data/eng`, a stub for supabase.

- Our statement runs from their last statement's lines plus our invoices after it. Our invoices dated in that statement's month (after the statement before it) that aren't on it are carried forward (⏳ "not on their … statement: carried here, waiting for their next one"), and checked against their next statement (✓ "now on their … one", or flagged "ask them about it"). $0 lines on our side are packing slips loaded before the invoice (📦, "the amount comes with their invoice"). `openOurStatement` (`late`).
- Supplier invoices → By year → a month under "Not on a statement yet" (e.g. November before their statement comes): it now has the same big button as a statement month, "📄 Open the November 2026 statement so far", which opens Our statement as of the end of that month, or today (`data-sinv-oursat`, `openOurStatement(sup, asOf)`). Every supplier (Carlo, Nov 9).
- A line on their statement we have no invoice for (or only its $0 packing slip): "🤖 Make our invoice from their statement" (asks Sure? first) makes/fills our invoice with their amount (before tax/HST worked out at 13%) and marks it "Made by the app from their statement… our copy was missing" (`read_notes`; shown purple 🤖 on Our statement and Supplier invoices). `supplier_invoice_from_statement`, `sync/extra/231_invoice_from_statement.sql`. Date by hand: 📅 Date / Save date on Supplier invoices (`230_supplier_invoice_date.sql`).
- Click any line of Our statement to fix it (blue box under it): the invoice's amount (with tax; before tax/HST redone at 13%, noted "Amount changed by hand") and date, and the payments in that statement (amount + why, `supplier_payment_edit`), since payments are taken off the oldest invoices first. `sync/extra/232_statement_fix.sql`.
- Credits are their own minus lines on Our statement (like theirs), matched to their credit lines; only payments are taken off invoices. Payments are taken off whole invoices only, oldest first; money left that would only part-pay an invoice is a "💳 Credit with <supplier>" box under Our statement (Carlo, Nov 9: it confused us on the lines), not taken off any line or the balance due.
- Lines carried from their last statement that are gone from their next one were paid: if we have no payment, a green "💵 Record the payment" box (date, how, amount; note "Made by the app…") adds it with `supplier_payment_add`.
- View Supplier Invoices (by month): click an invoice to change its 📅 date (it moves to that month) or amount (`openMonthInvoices`).
- Once a sent document is saved into Supplier invoices from the reader it leaves the Sent lists and can't be loaded again (`loaded_at`, `supplier_mail_loaded`, `233_supplier_mail_loaded.sql`).
